11
Conclusion

This paper provides a new, pooled minimum eigenvalue (PME), methodology for
the analysis of multiple long run relations in panel data models where the
cross section dimension,
n
, is large relative to the time series
dimension,
T
. It uses non-overlapping sub-sample time averages as
deviations from their full-sample counterpart and estimates the number of
long run relations and their coefficients using eigenvalues and eigenvectors
of the pooled covariance matrix of these sub-sample deviations. It applies
to unbalanced panels generated from general linear processes with
interactive stationary time effects and does not require knowing long run
causal linkages. The PME estimator is consistent and asymptotically normally
distributed as
n
and
T

\rightarrow\infty
jointly, such that
T\approx n^{d}
, with
d>0
for consistency and
d>1/2
for asymptotic
normality. Extensive Monte Carlo studies show that the number of long run
relations can be estimated with high precision and the PME estimates of the
long run coefficients show small bias and
RMSE
and have good size and
power properties. The utility of our approach is illustrated with an
application to key financial variables using an unbalanced panel of US firms
from merged CRSP-Compustat data set covering
2,000
plus firms over the
period
1950-2021
.

As well as application in other areas than corporate finance the procedure
opens up a range of theoretical developments. One question that we are
considering investigating is whether it is possible to develop a unit root
test based on similar principles. Our method is semi-parametric, in that it
does not model the short run dynamics. Because the estimates of the long run
relationships are super consistent, then having estimated them, the PME
estimates of the long run relations could be used as inputs into second
stage models using stationary variables. For instance, they can provide
estimates of the disequilibrium terms in error correction models of the
short run dynamics, to measure speeds of adjustment. Such models could also
be used for medium-term forecasting and counterfactual analysis.

Appendix A
Appendix

This appendix is organized in two sections. Section
A.1
presents lemmas
and their proofs. Section
A.2
provides proofs of propositions and
theorems stated in the paper. This appendix follows the same notations as in
Section
4
. Sub-sample time averages for a generic vector
\mathbf{x}_{it}
are defined as

\mathbf{\bar{x}}_{i\ell}=\frac{1}{T_{q}}\sum_{t=(\ell-1)T_{q}+1}^{\ell T_{q}}\mathbf{x}_{it},\text{ for }\ell=1,2,...,q\text{,}

and the full-sample time average by
\mathbf{\bar{x}}_{i\circ}=q^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}\mathbf{\bar{x}}_{i\ell}
, where it is assumed
that
m
is fixed,
q

(\geq 2)
is fixed, and
T_{q}=T/q
is an integer.
Small and large finite positive constants that do not depend on sample sizes

n
and
T
are denoted by
\epsilon
and
K
, respectively. They can take
different values at different instances.

A.1
Lemmas

Lemma A.1

Let
\mathbf{v}_{it}=\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\ast}(L)\mathbf{u}_{it}=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\mathbf{C}_{ij}^{\ast}\mathbf{u}_{i,t-j}
and
suppose Assumptions
1
and
2
hold. Then there exist finite
positive constants
K_{1}
,
K_{2}
, and
0<\rho<1
such that

\sup_{i}\left\|\mathbf{\Gamma}_{i}(h)\right\|<K_{1}\rho^{h}\text{,}

(A.1)

and

\sum_{h=0}^{\infty}\sup_{i}\left\|\mathbf{\Gamma}_{i}(h)\right\|<K_{2}\text{,}

(A.2)

where
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{i}(h)=E\left(\mathbf{v}_{it}\mathbf{v}_{i,t-h}^{\prime}\right),
for
h=0,1,2,...\,\
is the autocovariance
function of
\mathbf{v}_{it}
.

Proof.
The autocovariance function of
\mathbf{v}_{it}
is

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Gamma}_{i}(h)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\left(\mathbf{v}_{it}\mathbf{v}_{i,t-h}^{\prime}\right)=E\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\mathbf{C}_{ij}^{\ast}\mathbf{u}_{i,t-j}\right)\left(\sum_{j^{\prime}=0}^{\infty}\mathbf{u}_{i,t-j^{\prime}-h}^{\prime}\mathbf{C}_{ij^{\prime}}^{\ast\prime}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\sum_{j^{\prime}=0}^{\infty}\mathbf{C}_{ij}^{\ast}E\left(\mathbf{u}_{i,t-j}\mathbf{u}_{i,t-j^{\prime}-h}^{\prime}\right)\mathbf{C}_{ij^{\prime}}^{\ast\prime}=\sum_{j=h}^{\infty}\mathbf{C}_{ij}^{\ast}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\mathbf{C}_{i,j-h}^{\ast^{\prime}}=\mathbf{\Gamma}_{i}^{\prime}(-h).

Taking spectral norm and supremum over
i
yields

\sup_{i}\left\|\mathbf{\Gamma}_{i}(h)\right\|\leq\sum_{j=h}^{\infty}\sup_{i}\left\|\mathbf{C}_{ij}^{\ast}\right\|\sup_{i}\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\right\|\sup_{i}\left\|\mathbf{C}_{i,j-h}^{\ast}\right\|\leq K_{0}\sum_{j=h}^{\infty}\rho^{j}\rho^{j-h}=\frac{K_{0}\rho^{h}}{1-\rho^{2}}<K_{1}\rho^{h},

where
\sup_{i}\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\right\|<K
by Assumption
1
and
\sup_{i}\left\|\mathbf{C}_{ij}^{\ast}\right\|<K\rho^{j}
with
0<\rho<1
by Assumption
2
. It follows
\sum_{h=0}^{\infty}\sup_{i}\left\|\mathbf{\Gamma}_{i}(h)\right\|<K_{2}
.

Lemma A.2

Consider the
m\times m
matrix
\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{s}_{i}\bar{s}_{i}}=T^{-1}q^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}\left(\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\ell}-\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\circ}\right)\left(\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\ell}-\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\circ}\right)^{\prime}
, where
\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\ell}
and
\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\circ}
are sub-sample and full sample time averages of
the partial sum process
\mathbf{s}_{it}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{t}\mathbf{u}_{i\ell}
. Suppose
E(\mathbf{u}_{it}\mathbf{u}_{it}^{\prime})=\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}
for
i=1,2,...,n
and
t=1,2,...,T
, and
E(\mathbf{u}_{it}\mathbf{u}_{it^{\prime}}^{\prime})=\mathbf{0}
for
i=1,2,...,n
, and all
t\neq t^{\prime}
,
t,t^{\prime}=1,2,...,T
. Then

E\left(\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{s}_{i}\bar{s}_{i}}\right)=\frac{(q-1)}{6}\left(\frac{1}{q}+\frac{1}{T^{2}}\right)\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\text{, }

(A.3)

for
q\geq 2
and
i=1,2,...,n
.

Proof.
We note that

E\left(T\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{s}_{i}\bar{s}_{i}}\right)=E\left[q^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}\left(\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\ell}-\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\circ}\right)\left(\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\ell}-\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\circ}\right)^{\prime}\right]=q^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}E\left(\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\ell}\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\ell}^{\prime}\right)-E\left(\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\circ}\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\circ}^{\prime}\right)\text{.}

(A.4)

Also

\displaystyle\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T_{q}}\left(\mathbf{s}_{i1}+\mathbf{s}_{i2}+....+\mathbf{s}_{i,T_{q}}\right)=\frac{1}{T_{q}}\left[\mathbf{u}_{i1}+(\mathbf{u}_{i1}+\mathbf{u}_{i2})+....\left(\mathbf{u}_{i1}+\mathbf{u}_{i2}+...+\mathbf{u}_{i,T_{q}}\right)\right]\text{,}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T_{q}}\left[T_{q}\mathbf{u}_{i1}+(T_{q}-1)\mathbf{u}_{i2}+(T_{q}-2)\mathbf{u}_{i3}+....+2\mathbf{u}_{i,T_{q}-1}+\mathbf{u}_{i,T_{q}}\right]\text{,}

which can be written more compactly as
\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i1}=T_{q}^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{T_{q}}\left(T_{q}-t+1\right)\mathbf{u}_{it}
. Similarly

\displaystyle\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T_{q}}\left(\mathbf{s}_{i,T_{q}+1}+\mathbf{s}_{i,T_{q}+2}+....+\mathbf{s}_{i,2T_{q}}\right)=\frac{1}{T_{q}}\left[T_{q}\left(\mathbf{u}_{i1}+\mathbf{u}_{i2}+...+\mathbf{u}_{i,T_{q}}\right)+\sum_{t=T_{q}+1}^{2T_{q}}\left(2T_{q}-t+1\right)\mathbf{u}_{it}\right]\text{,}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T_{q}}\mathbf{u}_{it}+\frac{1}{T_{q}}\sum_{t=T_{q}+1}^{2T_{q}}\left(2T_{q}-t+1\right)\mathbf{u}_{it}\text{,}

and more generally

\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\ell}=\sum_{t=1}^{(\ell-1)T_{q}}\mathbf{u}_{it}+\frac{1}{T_{q}}\sum_{t=(\ell-1)T_{q}+1}^{\ell T_{q}}\left(\ell T_{q}+1-t\right)\mathbf{u}_{it}\text{, for }\ell=1,2,...,q\text{.}

(A.5)

It now readily follows that

E\left(\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\ell}\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\ell}^{\prime}\right)=\left[\left(\ell-1\right)T_{q}+\left(\frac{1}{T_{q}}\right)^{2}\sum_{t=\left(\ell-1\right)T_{q}+1}^{\ell T_{q}}\left(\ell T_{q}-t+1\right)^{2}\right]\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\text{.}

But

\sum_{t=\left(\ell-1\right)T_{q}+1}^{\ell T_{q}}\left(\ell T_{q}-t+1\right)^{2}=T_{q}^{2}+(T_{q}-1)^{2}+...+1=\frac{T_{q}(T_{q}+1)(2T_{q}+1)}{6}\text{,}

and we obtain

E\left(\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\ell}\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\ell}^{\prime}\right)=\left[\left(\ell-1\right)T_{q}+\left(\frac{1}{T_{q}}\right)^{2}\frac{T_{q}(T_{q}+1)(2T_{q}+1)}{6}\right]\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}=\left[\left(\ell-1\right)T_{q}+\frac{(T_{q}+1)(2T_{q}+1)}{6T_{q}}\right]\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\text{,}

and

q^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}E\left(\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\ell}\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\ell}^{\prime}\right)=q^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}\left(\left(\ell-1\right)T_{q}+\frac{(T_{q}+1)(2T_{q}+1)}{6T_{q}}\right)\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\text{.}

Noting that

q^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}\left(\ell-1\right)T_{q}=q^{-1}\left[T_{q}+2T_{q}+...+(q-1)T_{q}\right]=q^{-1}T_{q}\frac{q(q-1)}{2}=\frac{(q-1)T_{q}}{2}\text{,}

we have

q^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}E\left(\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\ell}\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\ell}^{\prime}\right)=\left[\frac{(q-1)T_{q}}{2}+\frac{(T_{q}+1)(2T_{q}+1)}{6T_{q}}\right]\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\text{.}

Recalling that
T_{q}=T/q
, we can write the expression above as

q^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}E\left(\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\ell}\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\ell}^{\prime}\right)=\left[\frac{(q-1)T}{2q}+\frac{(T+q)(2T+q)}{6qT}\right]\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\text{.}

(A.6)

Similarly, for the full sample average we have
\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\circ}=T^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(T-t+1\right)\mathbf{u}_{it}
, and

E\left(\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\circ}\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\circ}^{\prime}\right)=\frac{1}{T^{2}}\left[T^{2}+(T-1)^{2}+....+1\right]\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}=\frac{T(T+1)(2T+1)}{6T^{2}}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\text{.}

(A.7)

Using (
A.6
) and (
A.7
) in (
A.4
), yields

E\left(T\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{s}_{i}\bar{s}_{i}}\right)=\left[\frac{T(q-1)}{2q}+\frac{(T+q)(2T+q)}{6Tq}\right]\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}-\frac{(T+1)(2T+1)}{6T}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}=\frac{(q-1)}{6}\left(\frac{T}{q}+\frac{1}{T}\right)\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\text{,}

(A.8)

and result (
A.3
) follows.

Lemma A.3

Consider
m\times m
matrices
\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{v}_{i}\bar{v}_{i}}=T^{-1}q^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}\left(\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}-\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}\right)\left(\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}-\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}\right)^{\prime}
and

\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{s}_{i}\bar{v}_{i}}=T^{-1}q^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}\left(\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\ell}-\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\circ}\right)\left(\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}-\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}\right)^{\prime}
, where
\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\ell}
,
\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\circ}
are the sub-sample
and full sample time averages of the partial sum process
\mathbf{s}_{it}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{t}\mathbf{u}_{it},
where
m
and
q
(
\geq 2
) are
fixed. Also
\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}
and
\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}
are
the sub-sample and full sample time averages of
\mathbf{v}_{it}=\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\ast}(L)\mathbf{u}_{it}=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\mathbf{C}_{ij}^{\ast}\mathbf{u}_{i,t-j}
, and Assumptions
1
and
2
hold. Then

\sup_{i}E\left\|\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{v}_{i}\bar{v}_{i}}\right\|=O\left(T^{-2}\right),

(A.9)

\sup_{i}\left\|T^{2}\text{ }E\left(\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{v}_{i}\bar{v}_{i}}\right)\right\|=O\left(T^{-1}\right)+O\left(\rho^{T/q}\right)\text{, }

(A.10)

\sup_{i}E\left\|\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{s}_{i}\bar{v}_{i}}\right\|=O\left(T^{-1}\right)\text{,}

(A.11)

and

\sup_{i}\left\|E\left(\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{s}_{i}\bar{v}_{i}}\right)\right\|=O\left(T^{-2}\right)\text{.}

(A.12)

Proof.
Consider

T\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{v}_{i}\bar{v}_{i}}=q^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}\left(\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}-\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}\right)\left(\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}-\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}\right)^{\prime}=q^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}^{\prime}-\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}^{\prime}\text{,}

where
\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}=q^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}
. Taking spectral norm and expectations yields

E\left\|T\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{v}_{i}\bar{v}_{i}}\right\|\leq q^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}E\left\|\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}\right\|^{2}+E\left\|\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}\right\|^{2}\text{{,}}

and it follows

\sup_{i}E\left\|T\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{v}_{i}\bar{v}_{i}}\right\|\leq\sup_{i,\ell}E\left\|\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}\right\|^{2}+\sup_{i}E\left\|\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}\right\|^{2}\text{.}

(A.13)

Term
E\left\|\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}\right\|^{2}
can be written
as

E\left\|\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}\right\|^{2}=E\left(\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}^{\prime}\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}\right)=E\left[tr\left(\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}^{\prime}\right)\right]=tr\left[E\left(\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}^{\prime}\right)\right]\text{,}

where, under Assumptions
1
-
2
process
\mathbf{v}_{it}=\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\ast}(L)\mathbf{u}_{it}
is covariance-stationary with
uniformly (in
i
) absolutely summable autocovariances, and

E\left(\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}^{\prime}\right)=\frac{1}{T_{q}}\left\{\Gamma_{i}(0)+\sum_{h=1}^{T_{q}-1}\left(1-\frac{h}{T_{q}}\right)\left[\Gamma_{i}(h)+\Gamma_{i}^{\prime}(h)\right]\right\}\text{,}

(A.14)

in which
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{i}(h)=E\left(\mathbf{v}_{it}\mathbf{v}_{i,t-h}^{\prime}\right)
. Using Lemma
A.1
, it follows
\sup_{i,\ell}E\left\|\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}\right\|^{2}=O\left(T_{q}^{-1}\right)
, and given that
q
is fixed (does not change with the
sample size), then
T_{q}\approx T
, and we in turn obtain

\sup_{i,\ell}E\left\|\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}\right\|^{2}=O\left(T^{-1}\right)\text{.}

(A.15)

Similarly,
E\left\|\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}\right\|^{2}=tr\left[E\left(\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}^{\prime}\right)\right]
, where

E\left(\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}^{\prime}\right)=\frac{1}{T}\left\{\Gamma_{i}(0)+\sum_{h=1}^{T-1}\left(1-\frac{h}{T}\right)\left[\Gamma_{i}(h)+\Gamma_{i}^{\prime}(h)\right]\right\}\text{,}

(A.16)

and using Lemma
A.1
, we obtain

\sup_{i}E\left\|\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}\right\|^{2}=O\left(T^{-1}\right)\text{.}

(A.17)

Using (
A.15
) and (
A.17
) in (
A.13
) yields result (
A.9
).
Consider now (
A.10
) and note that

T\text{ }E\left(\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{v}_{i}\bar{v}_{i}}\right)=q^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}E\left(\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}^{\prime}\right)-E\left(\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}^{\prime}\right),

and upon using (
A.14
) we have (recall that
T_{q}=T/q)

q^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}E\left(\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}^{\prime}\right)=\frac{1}{T}\left\{\Gamma_{i}(0)+\sum_{h=1}^{T_{q}-1}\left(1-\frac{h}{T_{q}}\right)\left[\Gamma_{i}(h)+\Gamma_{i}^{\prime}(h)\right]\right\}.

Also using (
A.16
) we now have

\displaystyle T\text{ }E\left(\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{v}_{i}\bar{v}_{i}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{h=1}^{T_{q}-1}\left(1-\frac{h}{T_{q}}\right)\left[\Gamma_{i}(h)+\Gamma_{i}^{\prime}(h)\right]-\frac{1}{T}\sum_{h=1}^{T-1}\left(1-\frac{h}{T}\right)\left[\Gamma_{i}(h)+\Gamma_{i}^{\prime}(h)\right]\text{,}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{h=1}^{T/q-1}\left[\left(1-\frac{qh}{T}\right)-\left(1-\frac{h}{T}\right)\right]\left[\Gamma_{i}(h)+\Gamma_{i}^{\prime}(h)\right]-\frac{1}{T}\sum_{h=T/q+1}^{T-1}\left(1-\frac{h}{T}\right)\left[\Gamma_{i}(h)+\Gamma_{i}^{\prime}(h)\right]\text{.}

Hence, as required we have

T^{2}\text{ }E\left(\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{v}_{i}\bar{v}_{i}}\right)=\frac{\left(q-1\right)}{T}\sum_{h=1}^{T/q-1}h\left[\Gamma_{i}(h)+\Gamma_{i}^{\prime}(h)\right]-\sum_{h=T/q+1}^{T-1}\left(1-\frac{h}{T}\right)\left[\Gamma_{i}(h)+\Gamma_{i}^{\prime}(h)\right]\text{, and}

T^{2}\text{ }\left\|E\left(\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{v}_{i}\bar{v}_{i}}\right)\right\|\leq\frac{2\left(q-1\right)}{T}\sum_{h=1}^{T/q-1}h\left\|\Gamma_{i}(h)\right\|+\sum_{h=T/q+1}^{T-1}\left\|\Gamma_{i}(h)\right\|.

Since
\sup_{i}\left\|\mathbf{\Gamma}_{i}(h)\right\|<K_{1}\rho^{h}
, (see (
A.1
) in Lemma
A.1
) it now follows that

T^{2}\text{ sup}_{i}\left\|E\left(\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{v}_{i}\bar{v}_{i}}\right)\right\|\leq\frac{2\left(q-1\right)}{T}K_{1}\sum_{h=1}^{T/q-1}h\rho^{h}+K_{1}\sum_{h=T/q+1}^{T-1}\rho^{h}.

(A.18)

Also

\sum_{h=1}^{T/q-1}h\rho^{h}=\rho\left(\sum_{h=1}^{T/q-1}h\rho^{h-1}\right)=\rho\frac{d\left(\sum_{h=1}^{T/q-1}\rho^{h}\right)}{d\rho}=\rho\frac{d}{d\rho}\left(\frac{\rho-\rho^{T/q}}{1-\rho}\right)<K,

and
\sum_{h=T/q+1}^{T-1}\rho^{h}=\left(\rho^{T/q+1}-\rho^{T}\right)/\left(1-\rho\right)
. Using these results in (
A.18
) we have
T^{2}
sup
{}_{i}\left\|E\left(\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{v}_{i}\bar{v}_{i}}\right)\right\|=O\left(T^{-1}\right)+O\left(\rho^{T/q}\right),
as
required. We establish (
A.11
) next.

T\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{s}_{i}\bar{v}_{i}}=q^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}\left(\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\ell}-\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\circ}\right)\left(\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}-\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}\right)^{\prime}=q^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\ell}\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}^{\prime}-\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\circ}\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}^{\prime}\text{,}

where
\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}=q^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}
and similarly
\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\circ}=q^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\ell}
. Taking spectral norm yields
\left\|T\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{s}_{i}\bar{v}_{i}}\right\|\leq q^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}\left\|\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\ell}\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}^{\prime}\right\|\mathbf{+}\left\|\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\circ}\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}^{\prime}\right\|
.
By Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,

E\left\|T\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{s}_{i}\bar{v}_{i}}\right\|\leq q^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}\left(E\left\|\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\ell}\right\|^{2}\right)^{1/2}\left(E\left\|\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}\right\|^{2}\right)^{1/2}+\left(E\left\|\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\circ}\right\|^{2}\right)^{1/2}\left(E\left\|\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}\right\|^{2}\right)^{1/2}\text{. }

(A.19)

Assumption
1
stipulates
\sup_{i}\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\right\|<K
. Using this bound in (
A.6
) and (
A.7
)
yields

\sup_{i}E\left\|\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\circ}\right\|^{2}=\sup_{i}tr\left[E\left(\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\circ}\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\circ}^{\prime}\right)\right]=O\left(T\right)\text{, and similarly }\sup_{i,\ell}E\left\|\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\ell}\right\|^{2}=O\left(T\right)\text{.}

(A.20)

In addition, we have already established
\sup_{i,\ell}E\left\|\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}\right\|^{2}=O\left(T^{-1}\right)
(see (
A.15
))
and
\sup_{i}E\left\|\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}\right\|^{2}=O\left(T^{-1}\right)
(see (
A.17
)). Using these results in (
A.19
)
yields
\sup_{i}E\left\|T\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{s}_{i}\bar{v}_{i}}\right\|=O\left(1\right)
, which in turn implies result (
A.11
).

We establish (
A.12
) next. Taking expectation of
\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{s}_{i}\bar{v}_{i}}
, we have

E\left(\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{s}_{i}\bar{v}_{i}}\right)=T^{-1}q^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}\left[E\left(\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\ell}\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}^{\prime}\right)-E\left(\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\ell}\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}^{\prime}\right)-E\left(\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\circ}\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}^{\prime}\right)+E\left(\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\circ}\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}^{\prime}\right)\right]\text{,}

(A.21)

where
\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\ell}=\sum_{t=1}^{(\ell-1)T_{q}}\mathbf{u}_{it}+T_{q}^{-1}\sum_{t=(\ell-1)T_{q}+1}^{\ell T_{q}}\left(\ell T_{q}+1-t\right)\mathbf{u}_{it}
,
\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\circ}=T^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(T-t+1\right)\mathbf{u}_{it}
,

\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}=T_{q}^{-1}\sum_{t=(\ell-1)T_{q}+1}^{\ell T_{q}}\mathbf{v}_{it}
,
\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}=T^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{v}_{it}
, and
\mathbf{v}_{it}=\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\ast}(L)\mathbf{u}_{it}
with
\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\ast}(L)=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\mathbf{C}_{ij}^{\ast}L^{j}
.
We consider each of the four terms inside the summation operator on the
right side of (
A.21
) in turn. For
E\left(\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\ell}\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}^{\prime}\right)
, we have

E\left(\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\ell}\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}^{\prime}\right)=\frac{1}{T_{q}}\sum_{t=1}^{(\ell-1)T_{q}}\sum_{t^{\prime}=(\ell-1)T_{q}+1}^{\ell T_{q}}E\left(\mathbf{u}_{it}\mathbf{v}_{it^{\prime}}^{\prime}\right)+\frac{1}{T_{q}^{2}}\sum_{t=(\ell-1)T_{q}+1}^{\ell T_{q}}\sum_{t^{\prime}=(\ell-1)T_{q}+1}^{\ell T_{q}}\left(\ell T_{q}+1-t\right)E\left(\mathbf{u}_{it}\mathbf{v}_{it^{\prime}}^{\prime}\right)\text{.}

(A.22)

Noting that

E\left(\mathbf{u}_{it}\mathbf{v}_{it^{\prime}}^{\prime}\right)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\mathbf{C}_{i,t^{\prime}-t}^{\ast\prime}\text{, for }t\leq t^{\prime}\text{,}\\
\mathbf{0}\text{, for }t>t^{\prime}\text{,}\end{array}\right.

(A.23)

we obtain

\frac{1}{T_{q}}\sum_{t=1}^{(\ell-1)T_{q}}\sum_{t^{\prime}=(\ell-1)T_{q}+1}^{\ell T_{q}}E\left(\mathbf{u}_{it}\mathbf{v}_{it^{\prime}}^{\prime}\right)=\frac{1}{T_{q}}\sum_{t=1}^{(\ell-1)T_{q}}\sum_{t^{\prime}=(\ell-1)T_{q}+1}^{\ell T_{q}}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\mathbf{C}_{i,t^{\prime}-t}^{\ast\prime}\text{.}

Recalling that under Assumptions
1
and
2
,
\sup_{i}\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\right\|<K
and
\sup_{i}\left\|\mathbf{C}_{ij}^{\ast\prime}\right\|<K\rho^{j},

for
\rho<1
, then it follows that

\displaystyle\sup_{i}\left\|\frac{1}{T_{q}}\sum_{t=1}^{(\ell-1)T_{q}}\sum_{t^{\prime}=(\ell-1)T_{q}+1}^{\ell T_{q}}E\left(\mathbf{u}_{it}\mathbf{v}_{it^{\prime}}^{\prime}\right)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T_{q}}\sum_{t=1}^{(\ell-1)T_{q}}\sum_{t^{\prime}=(\ell-1)T_{q}+1}^{\ell T_{q}}\sup_{i}\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\right\|\sup_{i}\left\|\mathbf{C}_{i,t^{\prime}-t}^{\ast\prime}\right\|\text{,}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T_{q}}\sum_{t=1}^{(\ell-1)T_{q}}\sum_{t^{\prime}=(\ell-1)T_{q}+1}^{\ell T_{q}}K\rho^{t^{\prime}-t}\text{,}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{K}{T_{q}}\sum_{t=1}^{(\ell-1)T_{q}}\rho^{(\ell-1)T_{q}-t+1}\sum_{j=0}^{T_{q}}\rho^{j}\text{.}

But both sums
\sum_{j=0}^{T_{q}}\rho^{j}
and
\sum_{t=1}^{(\ell-1)T_{q}}\rho^{(\ell-1)T_{q}-t+1}
are bounded in
T
(recall
T_{q}=T/q

where
q
does not change with sample size and is fixed). Hence

\sup_{i}\left\|\frac{1}{T_{q}}\sum_{t=1}^{(\ell-1)T_{q}}\sum_{t^{\prime}=(\ell-1)T_{q}+1}^{\ell T_{q}}E\left(\mathbf{u}_{it}\mathbf{v}_{it^{\prime}}^{\prime}\right)\right\|=O\left(T^{-1}\right)\text{.}

(A.24)

For the second term on the right side of (
A.22
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T_{q}^{2}}\sum_{t=(\ell-1)T_{q}+1}^{\ell T_{q}}\sum_{t^{\prime}=(\ell-1)T_{q}+1}^{\ell T_{q}}\left(\ell T_{q}+1-t\right)E\left(\mathbf{u}_{it}\mathbf{v}_{it^{\prime}}^{\prime}\right)=

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T_{q}^{2}}\sum_{t=(\ell-1)T_{q}+1}^{\ell T_{q}}\left(\ell T_{q}+1-t\right)\left[\sum_{t^{\prime}=(\ell-1)T_{q}+1}^{t-1}E\left(\mathbf{u}_{it}\mathbf{v}_{it^{\prime}}^{\prime}\right)+\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{\ell T_{q}}E\left(\mathbf{u}_{it}\mathbf{v}_{it^{\prime}}^{\prime}\right)\right]\text{,}

and substituting (
A.23
), we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T_{q}^{2}}\sum_{t=(\ell-1)T_{q}+1}^{\ell T_{q}}\sum_{t^{\prime}=(\ell-1)T_{q}+1}^{\ell T_{q}}\left(\ell T_{q}+1-t\right)E\left(\mathbf{u}_{it}\mathbf{v}_{it^{\prime}}^{\prime}\right)

(A.25)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T_{q}^{2}}\sum_{t=(\ell-1)T_{q}+1}^{\ell T_{q}}\left(\ell T_{q}+1-t\right)\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{\ell T_{q}}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\mathbf{C}_{i,t^{\prime}-t}^{\ast\prime}=\frac{1}{T_{q}^{2}}\sum_{k=0}^{T_{q}-1}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\mathbf{C}_{ik}^{\ast\prime}\sum_{j=k+1}^{T_{q}}j\right)\text{,}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T_{q}^{2}}\sum_{k=0}^{T_{q}-1}\frac{\left(T_{q}+k+1\right)\left(T_{q}-k\right)}{2}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\mathbf{C}_{ik}^{\ast\prime}\text{.}

Using (
A.24
) and (
A.25
) in (
A.22
) yields

\sup_{i}\left\|E\left(\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\ell}\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}^{\prime}\right)-\frac{1}{T_{q}^{2}}\sum_{k=0}^{T_{q}-1}\frac{\left(T_{q}+k+1\right)\left(T_{q}-k\right)}{2}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\mathbf{C}_{ik}^{\ast\prime}\right\|=O\left(T^{-1}\right)\text{.}

(A.26)

Next consider
E\left(\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\ell}\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}^{\prime}\right)
, and note that

E\left(\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\ell}\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}^{\prime}\right)=\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{(\ell-1)T_{q}}\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{T}E\left(\mathbf{u}_{it}\mathbf{v}_{it^{\prime}}^{\prime}\right)+\frac{1}{T_{q}T}\sum_{t=(\ell-1)T_{q}+1}^{\ell T_{q}}\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{T}\left(\ell T_{q}+1-t\right)E\left(\mathbf{u}_{it}\mathbf{v}_{it^{\prime}}^{\prime}\right)\text{.}

(A.27)

For the first term on the right side of (
A.27
), we obtain

\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{(\ell-1)T_{q}}\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{T}E\left(\mathbf{u}_{it}\mathbf{v}_{it^{\prime}}^{\prime}\right)=\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{(\ell-1)T_{q}}\left[\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t-1}E\left(\mathbf{u}_{it}\mathbf{v}_{it^{\prime}}^{\prime}\right)+\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}E\left(\mathbf{u}_{it}\mathbf{v}_{it^{\prime}}^{\prime}\right)\right]=\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{(\ell-1)T_{q}}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\mathbf{C}_{i,t^{\prime}-t}^{\ast\prime}\text{,}

and using
k=t^{\prime}-t
, we have

\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{(\ell-1)T_{q}}\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{T}E\left(\mathbf{u}_{it}\mathbf{v}_{it^{\prime}}^{\prime}\right)=\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{(\ell-1)T_{q}}\sum_{k=0}^{T-t}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\mathbf{C}_{ik}^{\ast\prime}=\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{(\ell-1)T_{q}}\sum_{k=0}^{T_{q}-1}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\mathbf{C}_{ik}^{\ast\prime}+\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{(\ell-1)T_{q}}\sum_{k=T_{q}-1}^{T-t}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\mathbf{C}_{ik}^{\ast\prime}\text{,}

where (noting that
T=qT_{q}
)
T^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{(\ell-1)T_{q}}\sum_{k=0}^{T_{q}-1}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\mathbf{C}_{ik}^{\ast\prime}=q^{-1}\left(\ell-1\right)\sum_{k=0}^{T_{q}-1}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\mathbf{C}_{ik}^{\ast\prime}
, and (noting that
\left\|\mathbf{C}_{ij}^{\ast\prime}\right\|<K\rho^{j}
and
\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\right\|<K
)

\displaystyle\sup_{i}\left\|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{(\ell-1)T_{q}}\sum_{k=T_{q}-1}^{T-t}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\mathbf{C}_{ik}^{\ast\prime}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{(\ell-1)T_{q}}\sum_{k=T_{q}}^{T-t}\sup_{i}\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\right\|\sup_{i}\left\|\mathbf{C}_{ik}^{\ast\prime}\right\|<

\displaystyle\frac{K}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{(\ell-1)T_{q}}\sum_{k=T_{q}}^{T-t}\rho^{k}

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle K\frac{(\ell-1)T_{q}}{T}\rho^{T_{q}}\sum_{j=0}^{T-t-T_{q}}\rho^{j}<K_{2}\rho^{T_{q}}\text{,}

where
\left|\rho\right|<1
. Hence

\sup_{i}\left\|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{(\ell-1)T_{q}}\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{T}E\left(\mathbf{u}_{it}\mathbf{v}_{it^{\prime}}^{\prime}\right)-\frac{\ell-1}{q}\sum_{k=0}^{T_{q}-1}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\mathbf{C}_{ik}^{\ast\prime}\right\|=O\left(\rho^{T_{q}}\right)\text{.}

(A.28)

For the second term on the right side of (
A.27
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T_{q}T}\sum_{t=(\ell-1)T_{q}+1}^{\ell T_{q}}\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{T}\left(\ell T_{q}+1-t\right)E\left(\mathbf{u}_{it}\mathbf{v}_{it^{\prime}}^{\prime}\right)=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T_{q}T}\sum_{t=(\ell-1)T_{q}+1}^{\ell T_{q}}\left[\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t-1}\left(\ell T_{q}+1-t\right)E\left(\mathbf{u}_{it}\mathbf{v}_{it^{\prime}}^{\prime}\right)+\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\left(\ell T_{q}+1-t\right)E\left(\mathbf{u}_{it}\mathbf{v}_{it^{\prime}}^{\prime}\right)\right]\text{,}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T_{q}T}\sum_{t=(\ell-1)T_{q}+1}^{\ell T_{q}}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\left(\ell T_{q}+1-t\right)\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\mathbf{C}_{i,t^{\prime}-t}^{\ast\prime}\text{.}

Using
k=t^{\prime}-t
and
j=t-(\ell-1)T_{q}
, we obtain

\frac{1}{T_{q}T}\sum_{t=(\ell-1)T_{q}+1}^{\ell T_{q}}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\left(\ell T_{q}+1-t\right)\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\mathbf{C}_{i,t^{\prime}-t}^{\ast\prime}=\frac{1}{T_{q}T}\sum_{j=1}^{T_{q}}\sum_{k=0}^{T-(\ell-1)T_{q}-j}\left(T_{q}+1-j\right)\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\mathbf{C}_{ik}^{\ast\prime}\text{.}

Consider the case
\ell<q
and
\ell=q
in turn. For
\ell<q
, we can
write

\sum_{k=0}^{T-(\ell-1)T_{q}-j}\mathbf{C}_{ik}^{\ast\prime}=\sum_{k=0}^{T_{q}-1}\mathbf{C}_{ik}^{\ast\prime}+\sum_{k=T_{q}}^{T-(\ell-1)T_{q}-j}\mathbf{C}_{ik}^{\ast\prime}\text{,}

where

\sup_{i}\left\|\sum_{k=T_{q}}^{T-(\ell-1)T_{q}-j}\mathbf{C}_{ik}^{\ast\prime}\right\|\leq\sum_{k=T_{q}}^{T-(\ell-1)T_{q}-j}\sup_{i}\left\|\mathbf{C}_{ik}^{\ast\prime}\right\|<K\sum_{k=T_{q}}^{T-(\ell-1)T_{q}-j}\rho^{k}<K_{3}\rho^{T_{q}}\text{,}

since
\left|\rho\right|<1
. Hence, for
\ell<q
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T_{q}T}\sum_{j=1}^{T_{q}}\sum_{k=0}^{T-(\ell-1)T_{q}-j}\left(T_{q}+1-j\right)\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\mathbf{C}_{ik}^{\ast\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T_{q}T}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\sum_{j=1}^{T_{q}}\left[\left(T_{q}+1-j\right)\left(\sum_{k=0}^{T_{q}-1}\mathbf{C}_{ik}^{\ast\prime}+O\left(\rho^{T_{q}}\right)\right)\right]\text{,}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T_{q}T}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\sum_{j=1}^{T_{q}}\sum_{k=0}^{T_{q}-1}\left(T_{q}+1-j\right)\mathbf{C}_{ik}^{\ast\prime}+O\left(\rho^{T_{q}}\right)\text{,}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T_{q}T}\frac{\left(T_{q}+1\right)T_{q}}{2}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\sum_{k=0}^{T_{q}-1}\mathbf{C}_{ik}^{\ast\prime}+O\left(\rho^{T_{q}}\right)\text{.}

where the
O\left(\rho^{T_{q}}\right)
term is uniform in
i
. Noting
that

\frac{1}{T_{q}T}\frac{\left(T_{q}+1\right)T_{q}}{2}=\frac{1}{2q}+O\left(T^{-1}\right)\text{,}

and using the fact that upper bound
O\left(\rho^{T_{q}}\right)\,
is
dominated by
O\left(T^{-1}\right)
, we obtain (for
\ell<q
)

\frac{1}{T_{q}T}\sum_{t=(\ell-1)T_{q}+1}^{\ell T_{q}}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\left(\ell T_{q}+1-t\right)\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\mathbf{C}_{i,t^{\prime}-t}^{\ast\prime}=\frac{1}{2q}\sum_{k=0}^{T_{q}-1}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\mathbf{C}_{ik}^{\ast\prime}+O\left(T^{-1}\right)\text{,}

where the
O\left(T^{-1}\right)
term is uniform in
i
. For
\ell=q
, we
have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T_{q}T}\sum_{j=1}^{T_{q}}\sum_{k=0}^{T-H_{q-1}-j}\left(T_{q}+1-j\right)\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\mathbf{C}_{ik}^{\ast\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T_{q}T}\sum_{j=1}^{T_{q}}\sum_{k=0}^{T_{q}-j}\left(T_{q}+1-j\right)\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\mathbf{C}_{ik}^{\ast\prime}\text{,}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T_{q}T}\sum_{k=0}^{T_{q}-1}\frac{\left(T_{q}+k+1\right)\left(T_{q}-k\right)}{2}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\mathbf{C}_{ik}^{\ast\prime}\text{.}

But

\frac{1}{T_{q}T}\sum_{k=0}^{T_{q}-1}\frac{\left(T_{q}+k+1\right)\left(T_{q}-k\right)}{2}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\mathbf{C}_{ik}^{\ast\prime}=\frac{1}{T_{q}T}\sum_{k=0}^{T_{q}-1}\frac{\left(T_{q}+1\right)T_{q}}{2}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\mathbf{C}_{ik}^{\ast\prime}-\frac{1}{T_{q}T}\sum_{k=0}^{T_{q}-1}\frac{\left(k+1\right)k}{2}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\mathbf{C}_{ik}^{\ast\prime}\text{,}

where the second term can be bounded by

\sup_{i}\left\|\frac{1}{T_{q}T}\sum_{k=1}^{T_{q}-j}\frac{\left(k+1\right)k}{2}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\mathbf{C}_{ik}^{\ast\prime}\right\|<\frac{K}{T_{q}T}\sum_{k=1}^{T_{q}-j}\frac{\left(k+1\right)k}{2}\rho^{k}=O\left(T^{-1}\right)\text{.}

Hence regardless of
\ell=q
or
\ell<q
, we have

\sup_{i}\left\|\frac{1}{T_{q}T}\sum_{t=(\ell-1)T_{q}+1}^{\ell T_{q}}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\left(\ell T_{q}+1-t\right)\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\mathbf{C}_{i,t^{\prime}-t}^{\ast\prime}-\frac{1}{2q}\sum_{k=0}^{T_{q}-1}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\mathbf{C}_{ik}^{\ast\prime}\right\|=O\left(T^{-1}\right)

(A.29)

Using (
A.29
) and (
A.28
) in (
A.27
) and noting that
O\left(\rho^{T_{q}}\right)\,
is dominated by
O\left(T^{-1}\right)
, we
obtain

\sup_{i}\left\|E\left(\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\ell}\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}\right)-\frac{\ell-1}{q}\sum_{k=0}^{T_{q}-1}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\mathbf{C}_{ik}^{\ast\prime}-\frac{1}{2q}\sum_{k=0}^{T_{q}-1}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\mathbf{C}_{ik}^{\ast\prime}\right\|=O\left(T^{-1}\right)\text{.}

(A.30)

Consider the term
E\left(\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\circ}\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}^{\prime}\right)
next.

\displaystyle E\left(\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\circ}\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{TT_{q}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{t^{\prime}=(\ell-1)T_{q}+1}^{\ell T_{q}}\left(T-t+1\right)E\left(\mathbf{u}_{it}\mathbf{v}_{it^{\prime}}^{\prime}\right)\text{,}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{TT_{q}}\sum_{t^{\prime}=(\ell-1)T_{q}+1}^{\ell T_{q}}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{t^{\prime}}\left(T-t+1\right)E\left(\mathbf{u}_{it}\mathbf{v}_{it^{\prime}}^{\prime}\right)+\sum_{t=t^{\prime}+1}^{T}\left(T-t+1\right)\left(\mathbf{u}_{it}\mathbf{v}_{it^{\prime}}^{\prime}\right)\right]\text{,}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{TT_{q}}\sum_{t^{\prime}=(\ell-1)T_{q}+1}^{\ell T_{q}}\sum_{t=1}^{t^{\prime}}\left(T-t+1\right)\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\mathbf{C}_{i,t^{\prime}-t}^{\ast\prime}\text{.}

Using
k=t^{\prime}-t
and
j=t^{\prime}-H_{s-1}-1
, we obtain

\displaystyle E\left(\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\circ}\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{TT_{q}}\sum_{j=0}^{T_{q}-1}\sum_{k=0}^{j+(\ell-1)T_{q}}\left(T-(\ell-1)T_{q}-j+k\right)\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\mathbf{C}_{ik}^{\ast\prime}\text{,}

(A.31)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{TT_{q}}\sum_{j=0}^{T_{q}-1}\left(\sum_{k=0}^{(\ell-1)T_{q}}\left(T-(\ell-1)T_{q}-j+k\right)\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\mathbf{C}_{ik}^{\ast\prime}+\sum_{k=(\ell-1)T_{q}+1}^{j+(\ell-1)T_{q}}\left[T-(\ell-1)T_{q}-j+k\right]\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\mathbf{C}_{ik}^{\ast\prime}\right)\text{.}

But

\displaystyle\sup_{i}\left\|\sum_{k=(\ell-1)T_{q}+1}^{j+(\ell-1)T_{q}}\left(T-(\ell-1)T_{q}-j+k\right)\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\mathbf{C}_{ik}^{\ast\prime}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{k=(\ell-1)T_{q}+1}^{j+(\ell-1)T_{q}}\left(T-(\ell-1)T_{q}-j+k\right)\sup_{i}\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\right\|\sup_{i}\left\|\mathbf{C}_{ik}^{\ast\prime}\right\|\text{,}

(A.32)

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle KT\sum_{k=(\ell-1)T_{q}+1}^{j+(\ell-1)T_{q}}\rho^{k}<K_{1}\rho_{1}^{T}\text{,}

where
\left|\rho\right|<\rho_{1}<1
. In addition,

\left\|\frac{1}{TT_{q}}\sum_{j=0}^{T_{q}-1}\sum_{k=0}^{(\ell-1)T_{q}}k\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\mathbf{C}_{ik}^{\ast\prime}\right\|\leq\frac{1}{TT_{q}}\sum_{j=0}^{T_{q}-1}\sum_{k=0}^{(\ell-1)T_{q}}k\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\right\|\left\|\mathbf{C}_{ik}^{\ast\prime}\right\|<\frac{K}{TT_{q}}\sum_{j=0}^{T_{q}-1}\sum_{k=0}^{(\ell-1)T_{q}}k\rho^{k}\text{.}

Since
\sum_{k=0}^{(\ell-1)T_{q}}k\rho^{k}<K
, we obtain

\sup_{i}\left\|\frac{1}{TT_{q}}\sum_{j=0}^{T_{q}-1}\sum_{k=0}^{(\ell-1)T_{q}}k\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\mathbf{C}_{ik}^{\ast\prime}\right\|=O\left(T^{-1}\right)\text{.}

(A.33)

Using (
A.32
) and (
A.33
) in (
A.31
), it follows

\sup_{i}\left\|E\left(\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\circ}\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}\right)-\frac{1}{TT_{q}}\sum_{j=0}^{T_{q}-1}\sum_{k=0}^{(\ell-1)T_{q}}\left(T-(\ell-1)T_{q}-j\right)\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\mathbf{C}_{ik}^{\ast\prime}\right\|=O\left(T^{-1}\right)\text{.}

Given that

\frac{1}{TT_{q}}\sum_{j=0}^{T_{q}-1}\left(T-(\ell-1)T_{q}-j\right)=\frac{\left(2T-2(\ell-1)T_{q}-T_{q}+1\right)T_{q}}{2TT_{q}}=1-\frac{(\ell-1)T_{q}}{T}-\frac{T_{q}}{2T}+\frac{1}{2T}=1-\frac{\ell-1}{q}-\frac{1}{2q}+O\left(T^{-1}\right)\text{,}

it further follows

\sup_{i}\left\|E\left(\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\circ}\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}\right)-\left(1-\frac{\ell-1}{q}-\frac{1}{2q}\right)\sum_{k=0}^{(\ell-1)T_{q}}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\mathbf{C}_{ik}^{\ast\prime}\right\|=O\left(T^{-1}\right)\text{.}

(A.34)

For the purpose of this proof, it is convenient to decompose

\sum_{k=0}^{(\ell-1)T_{q}}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\mathbf{C}_{ik}^{\ast\prime}=\sum_{k=0}^{T_{q}-1}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\mathbf{C}_{ik}^{\ast\prime}+\sum_{k=T_{q}}^{(\ell-1)T_{q}}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\mathbf{C}_{ik}^{\ast\prime}\text{,}

where, as before,

\sup_{i}\left\|\sum_{k=T_{q}}^{(\ell-1)T_{q}}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\mathbf{C}_{ik}^{\ast\prime}\right\|\leq\sum_{k=T_{q}}^{(\ell-1)T_{q}}\sup_{i}\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\right\|\sup_{i}\left\|\mathbf{C}_{ik}^{\ast\prime}\right\|<K\sum_{k=T_{q}}^{(\ell-1)T_{q}}\rho^{k}<K_{1}\rho^{T_{q}}\text{.}

Using this result in (
A.34
) yields

\sup_{i}\left\|E\left(\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\circ}\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}^{\prime}\right)-\left(1-\frac{\ell-1}{q}-\frac{1}{2q}\right)\sum_{k=0}^{T_{q}-1}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\mathbf{C}_{ik}^{\ast\prime}\right\|=O\left(T^{-1}\right)\text{.}

(A.35)

Finally for the last term,
E\left(\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\circ}\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}^{\prime}\right)
, we have

\displaystyle E\left(\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\circ}\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T^{2}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{T}\left(T-t+1\right)E\left(\mathbf{u}_{it}\mathbf{v}_{it^{\prime}}^{\prime}\right)\text{,}

(A.36)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T^{2}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(T-t+1\right)\left[\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t-1}E\left(\mathbf{u}_{it}\mathbf{v}_{it^{\prime}}^{\prime}\right)+\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}E\left(\mathbf{u}_{it}\mathbf{v}_{it^{\prime}}^{\prime}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T^{2}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(T-t+1\right)\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\mathbf{C}_{i,t^{\prime}-t}^{\ast\prime}\text{,}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T^{2}}\sum_{k=0}^{T-1}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\mathbf{C}_{ik}^{\ast\prime}\sum_{j=k+1}^{T}j\right)\text{,}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T^{2}}\sum_{k=0}^{T-1}\frac{\left(T+k+1\right)\left(T-k\right)}{2}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\mathbf{C}_{ik}^{\ast\prime}\text{.}

It is further useful to split the sum above into first
T_{q}
terms and the
remainder, which can be appropriately bounded, namely

\frac{1}{T^{2}}\sum_{k=0}^{T-1}\frac{\left(T+k+1\right)\left(T-k\right)}{2}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\mathbf{C}_{ik}^{\ast\prime}=\frac{1}{T^{2}}\sum_{k=0}^{T_{q}-1}\frac{\left(T+k+1\right)\left(T-k\right)}{2}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\mathbf{C}_{ik}^{\ast\prime}+\frac{1}{T^{2}}\sum_{k=T_{q}}^{T-1}\frac{\left(T+k+1\right)\left(T-k\right)}{2}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\mathbf{C}_{ik}^{\ast\prime}\text{,}

where
11
11

11

We have also used that
\left(T+k+1\right)\left(T-k\right)/2T^{2}<K
.

\displaystyle\sup_{i}\left\|\frac{1}{T^{2}}\sum_{k=T_{q}}^{T-1}\frac{\left(T+k+1\right)\left(T-k\right)}{2}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\mathbf{C}_{ik}^{\ast\prime}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{k=T_{q}}^{T-1}\frac{\left(T+k+1\right)\left(T-k\right)}{2T^{2}}\sup_{i}\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\right\|\sup_{i}\left\|\mathbf{C}_{ik}^{\ast\prime}\right\|\text{,}

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle K\sum_{k=T_{q}}^{T-1}\rho^{k}<K_{1}\rho^{T_{q}}\text{.}

Hence, we obtain for the last term
E\left(\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\circ}\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}^{\prime}\right)
,

\sup_{i}\left\|E\left(\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\circ}\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}\right)-\frac{1}{T^{2}}\sum_{k=0}^{T_{q}-1}\frac{\left(T+k+1\right)\left(T-k\right)}{2}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\mathbf{C}_{ik}^{\ast\prime}\right\|=O\left(\rho^{T_{q}}\right)\text{.}

(A.37)

Consider now
E\left(\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\ell}\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}^{\prime}\right)-E\left(\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\ell}\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}^{\prime}\right)-E\left(\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\circ}\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}^{\prime}\right)+E\left(\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\circ}\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}^{\prime}\right)
. Using (
A.26
), (
A.30
), (
A.35
), and (
A.37
), we obtain

\sup_{i}\left\|E\left(\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\ell}\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}^{\prime}\right)-E\left(\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\ell}\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}^{\prime}\right)-E\left(\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\circ}\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}^{\prime}\right)+E\left(\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\circ}\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}^{\prime}\right)\right\|_{1}<\sup_{i}\left\|\sum_{k=0}^{T_{q}-1}a_{k}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\mathbf{C}_{ik}^{\ast\prime}\right\|+\frac{K}{T}\text{,}

where the coefficients
a_{k}
, for
k=0,1,...,T_{q}-1
, are given by

\displaystyle a_{k}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\left(T_{q}+k+1\right)\left(T_{q}-k\right)}{2T_{q}^{2}}-\left(\frac{\ell-1}{q}+\frac{1}{2q}\right)-\left(1-\frac{\ell-1}{q}-\frac{1}{2q}\right)+\frac{\left(T+k+1\right)\left(T-k\right)}{2T^{2}}\text{,}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{T_{q}-k-k^{2}}{2T_{q}^{2}}+\frac{T-k-k^{2}}{2T^{2}}=k\left(\frac{1}{2kT_{q}}-\frac{1}{2T_{q}^{2}}-\frac{k}{2T_{q}^{2}}+\frac{1}{2Tk}-\frac{1}{2T^{2}}-\frac{k}{2T^{2}}\right)\text{,}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{k}{T}\left(\frac{q}{2k}-\frac{q}{2T_{q}}-\frac{kq}{2T_{q}}+\frac{1}{2k}-\frac{1}{2T}-\frac{k}{2T}\right)\text{.}

It follows that the absolute value of coefficients
a_{k}
is bounded by

\left|a_{k}\right|<k\frac{K}{T}\text{,}

and using this bound we obtain

\sup_{i}\left\|\sum_{k=0}^{T_{q}-1}a_{k}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\mathbf{C}_{ik}^{\ast\prime}\right\|<\sum_{k=0}^{T_{q}-1}\left|a_{k}\right|\sup_{i}\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\right\|\sup_{i}\left\|\mathbf{C}_{ik}^{\ast\prime}\right\|<\frac{K}{T}\sum_{k=0}^{T_{q}-1}k\rho^{k}=O\left(T^{-1}\right)\text{.}

Hence

\sup_{i}\left\|E\left(\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\ell}\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}^{\prime}\right)-E\left(\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\ell}\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}^{\prime}\right)-E\left(\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\circ}\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}^{\prime}\right)+E\left(\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\circ}\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}^{\prime}\right)\right\|=O\left(T^{-1}\right)\text{,}

and using this bound in (
A.21
) yields
\sup_{i}\left\|E\left(\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{s}_{i}\bar{v}_{i}}\right)\right\|_{1}=O\left(T^{-2}\right)
, as required. This completes the proof of (
A.12
).

Lemma A.4

Suppose Assumptions
1
and
2
hold,
m
and
q

\left(\geq 2\right)
are fixed, and consider
\mathbf{\tilde{\xi}}_{iq}=\mathbf{\bar{\xi}}_{iq}-E\left(\mathbf{\bar{\xi}}_{iq}\right)
, where
\mathbf{\bar{\xi}}_{iq}=q^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}\left(\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}\mathbf{\otimes\bar{s}}_{i\ell}\right)-\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}\mathbf{\otimes\bar{s}}_{i\circ}
,
\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\ell}
and
\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\circ}
are, respectively, the sub-sample and full
sample time averages of the partial sum process
\mathbf{s}_{it}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{t}\mathbf{u}_{it}
, and
\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}
and
\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}
are, respectively, the sub-sample and full sample time averages
of
\mathbf{v}_{it}=\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\ast}(L)\mathbf{u}_{it}=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\mathbf{C}_{ij}^{\ast}\mathbf{u}_{i,t-j}
. Let
\kappa=\left(4+\epsilon\right)/2>2
, where
\epsilon>0
is the constant from
Assumption
1
. Then

\sup_{i,k,T}E\left|\tilde{\xi}_{iqk}\right|^{\kappa}<K\text{,}

(A.38)

\sup_{i,\ell,T}E\left\|\sqrt{T}\left(\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}-\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}\right)\right\|^{2\kappa}<K\text{,}

(A.39)

and

\sup_{i,\ell,T}\left\|T^{-1/2}\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\ell}\right\|^{2\kappa}<K\text{,}

(A.40)

where
\tilde{\xi}_{iqk}
is
k
-th element of
\mathbf{\tilde{\xi}}_{iq}
.

Proof.
Denote the individual elements of
\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}
and
\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\ell}
by
\bar{v}_{i\ell k}
and
\bar{s}_{i\ell k}
, for
k=1,2,...,m
,
respectively. These vectors depend on
T_{q}
but the
subscript
T_{q}
is suppressed to simplify notations. Given that
m
and
q

are fixed, sufficient condition for (
A.38
) is

\sup_{i,\ell,\ell^{\prime},k,k^{\prime},T}E\left|\bar{v}_{i\ell k}\bar{s}_{i\ell^{\prime}k^{\prime}}\right|^{\kappa}<K\text{,}

(A.41)

where
\kappa=\left(4+\epsilon\right)/2>2
since
\epsilon>0
by
Assumption
1
. It is convenient to write
\bar{v}_{i\ell k}\bar{s}_{i\ell^{\prime}k^{\prime}}
as
\left(T^{1/2}\bar{v}_{i\ell k}\right)\left(T^{-1/2}\bar{s}_{i\ell^{\prime}k^{\prime}}\right)
. Using
Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we obtain

E\left|\left(\sqrt{T}\bar{v}_{i\ell k}\right)\frac{\bar{s}_{i\ell^{\prime}k^{\prime}}}{\sqrt{T}}\right|^{\kappa}\leq\left(E\left|\sqrt{T}\bar{v}_{i\ell k}\right|^{2\kappa}\right)^{1/2}\left(E\left|\frac{\bar{s}_{i\ell^{\prime}k^{\prime}}}{\sqrt{T}}\right|^{2\kappa}\right)^{1/2}\text{.}

(A.42)

Consider
\sqrt{T}\bar{v}_{i\ell k}
first, and note that it can be written
as

\displaystyle\sqrt{T}\bar{v}_{i\ell k}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{T}T_{q}^{-1}\sum_{t=(\ell-1)T_{q}+1}^{\ell T_{q}}\mathbf{e}_{k}^{\prime}\mathbf{v}_{it}=qT^{-1/2}\sum_{t=(\ell-1)T_{q}+1}^{\ell T_{q}}\sum_{h=0}^{\infty}\mathbf{e}_{k}^{\prime}\mathbf{C}_{ih}^{\ast}\mathbf{u}_{i,t-h}\text{,}

(A.43)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle q\sum_{h=0}^{\infty}\mathbf{e}_{k}^{\prime}\mathbf{C}_{ih}^{\ast}\left(T^{-1/2}\sum_{t=(\ell-1)T_{q}+1}^{\ell T_{q}}\mathbf{u}_{i,t-h}\right)=q\sum_{h=0}^{\infty}\mathbf{e}_{k}^{\prime}\mathbf{C}_{ih}^{\ast}\left(T^{-1/2}\mathbf{\vartheta}_{i\ell h,T}\right)\text{,}

where
\mathbf{e}_{k}
is
m\times 1
selection vector for
k
-th element,
and
\mathbf{\vartheta}_{i\ell h,T}=\sum_{t=(\ell-1)T/q+1}^{\ell T/q}\mathbf{u}_{i,t-h}
is sum of
T_{q}=T/q
independent random vectors
distributed with zero means. Under Assumption
1
,
\sup_{it}E\left\|\mathbf{u}_{it}\right\|^{2\kappa}=\sup_{it}E\left\|\mathbf{u}_{it}\right\|^{4+\epsilon}<K
. Hence,
using the result on moments of the sums of independent random variables
given by equation (2) of

?
),
12
12

12

Let
X_{1},X_{2},...,X_{n}
be independent random variables and let
S_{n}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}X_{\ell}
. If
E\left(X_{\ell}\right)=0
for
\ell=1,2,...,n
, and
p\geq 2
then
E\left|S_{n}\right|^{p}\leq c\left(p\right)n^{p/2-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}E\left|X_{\ell}\right|^{p}
, where
c\left(p\right)>0
depends only on
p
. See

?
).
we have (for a fixed
q
)
\sup_{i\ell hT}E\left\|\mathbf{\vartheta}_{i\ell h,T}\right\|^{2\kappa}=O\left(T^{\kappa}\right)
, and it
follows

\sup_{i\ell hT}E\left\|T^{-1/2}\mathbf{\vartheta}_{i\ell h,T}\right\|^{2\kappa}<K\text{,}

(A.44)

Consider
E\left\|\sqrt{T}\bar{v}_{i\ell k}\right\|^{2\kappa}

next. Using (
A.43
) and the Minkowski inequality, we obtain the
following upper bound

\displaystyle\left(E\left\|\sqrt{T}\bar{v}_{i\ell k}\right\|^{2\kappa}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\kappa}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle q\sum_{h=0}^{\infty}\left[E\left\|\mathbf{e}_{k}^{\prime}\mathbf{C}_{ih}^{\ast}\left(T^{-1/2}\mathbf{\vartheta}_{i\ell h,T}\right)\right\|^{2\kappa}\right]^{\frac{1}{2\kappa}}\text{,}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle q\sum_{h=0}^{\infty}\left\|\mathbf{C}_{ih}^{\ast}\right\|\left(E\left\|T^{-1/2}\mathbf{\vartheta}_{i\ell h,T}\right\|^{2\kappa}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\kappa}}\text{.}

Using (
A.44
) and noting that under Assumption
2

\sup_{ih}\sum_{h=0}^{\infty}\left\|\mathbf{C}_{ih}^{\ast}\right\|<K
, we obtain

\sup_{i\ell kT}E\left\|\sqrt{T}\bar{v}_{i\ell k}\right\|^{2\kappa}<K\text{.}

(A.45)

Existence of a uniform bound for
E\left|T^{-1/2}\bar{s}_{i\ell k}\right|^{2\kappa}
is established next in a similar way. Using (
A.5
),
s_{i\ell k}
is given by

s_{i\ell k}=\sum_{t=1}^{(\ell-1)T_{q}}u_{ikt}+\frac{1}{T_{q}}\sum_{t=(\ell-1)T_{q}+1}^{\ell T_{q}}\left(\ell T_{q}+1-t\right)u_{ikt}=\sum_{t=1}^{\ell T_{q}}\zeta_{i\ell kt,T}\text{,}

where
\zeta_{i\ell kt,T}=a_{t\ell,T}u_{ikt}

a_{t\ell,T}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}1&\text{for }t=1,2,...,\left(\ell-1\right)T_{q}\\
T_{q}^{-1}\left(\ell T_{q}+1-t\right)&\text{for }t=(\ell-1)T_{q}+1,...,\ell T_{q}\end{array}\right.\text{,}

and we have
\left|a_{t\ell,T}\right|\leq 1
. Hence
s_{i\ell k}

is sum of independent random variables distributed with zero means. In
addition, Assumption
1
and
\left|a_{t\ell,T}\right|\leq 1
imply
\sup_{i\ell ktT}E\left|\zeta_{i\ell kt,T}\right|^{2\kappa}
. Using equation (2) of

?
)
we obtain
\sup_{i\ell kT}E\left|\bar{s}_{i\ell k}\right|^{2\kappa}=O\left(T^{\kappa}\right)
, and it follows

\sup_{i\ell kT}E\left|\frac{\bar{s}_{i\ell k}}{\sqrt{T}}\right|^{2\kappa}<K\text{.}

(A.46)

Using (
A.45
) and (
A.46
) in (
A.42
) establishes (
A.41
). This
completes the proof of (
A.38
). Consider (
A.39
) next. Given that
m

and
q
are fixed, sufficient condition for (
A.39
) to hold is (
A.45
), which was already established. Similarly, the last result of this lemma, (
A.40
), follows from (
A.46
). This completes the proof.

Lemma A.5

Consider
\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{v}\bar{v}}=T^{-1}n^{-1}q^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}\left(\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}-\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}\right)\left(\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}-\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}\right)^{\prime}
and suppose Assumptions
1
and
2
hold,
q\left(\geq 2\right)
is fixed and
m
is fixed. Let
\kappa=\left(4+\epsilon\right)/2>2
, where
\epsilon>0
is the constant from
Assumption
1
. Then for any
m\times 1
vector
\mathbf{b}
of unit
length,
\left\|\mathbf{b}\right\|=1
, we have

E\left(\left|\mathbf{b}^{\prime}\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{v}\bar{v}}\mathbf{b}\right|^{\kappa}\right)=O\left(n^{-\kappa/2}T^{-2\kappa}\right)\text{.}

(A.47)

Proof.
Denote
\left(k,\ell\right)
-th element of the scaled
m\times m
matrix
\sqrt{n}T^{2}\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{v}\bar{v}}
as
\tilde{q}_{vv,k\ell}
, which
can be written as

\tilde{q}_{vv,k\ell}=n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\nu_{ik}\nu_{i\ell}\text{,}

where
\nu_{ik}
is
k
-th element of
q^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}\sqrt{T}\left(\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}-\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}\right)
.
Consider
\nu_{ik}\nu_{i\ell}
. Using Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,

E\left|\nu_{ik}\nu_{i\ell}\right|^{\kappa}\leq\left(E\left|\nu_{ik}\right|^{2\kappa}\right)^{1/2}\left(E\left|\nu_{i\ell}\right|^{2\kappa}\right)^{1/2}\text{,}

and by result (
A.39
) of Lemma
A.4
, we obtain
E\left|\nu_{ik}\nu_{i\ell}\right|^{\kappa}
is uniformly bounded, namely

\sup_{k,\ell}E\left|\nu_{ik}\nu_{i\ell}\right|^{\kappa}<K\text{.}

Since
\nu_{ik}\nu_{i\ell}
is independently distributed over
i
, we can
use the result on moments of the sums of independent random variables given
by equation (2) of

?
), and obtain
E\left|\tilde{q}_{vv,k\ell}\right|^{\kappa}=O\left(1\right)
. This implies
E\left(\left|\mathbf{b}^{\prime}\left(\sqrt{n}T^{2}\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{v}\bar{v}}\right)\mathbf{b}\right|^{\kappa}\right)=O\left(1\right)
for any
m\times 1
vector

\mathbf{b}
of unit length. Result (
A.47
) now readily follows.

Lemma A.6

Consider
\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{w}\bar{w}}=T^{-1}n^{-1}q^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}\left(\mathbf{\bar{w}}_{i\ell}-\mathbf{\bar{w}}_{i\circ}\right)\left(\mathbf{\bar{w}}_{i\ell}-\mathbf{\bar{w}}_{i\circ}\right)^{\prime}
and suppose the
m\times 1
vector
\mathbf{w}_{it}
is given by (
1
) without interactive time effects (
\mathbf{G}_{i}=\mathbf{0}
), Assumptions
1
to
3
hold,
q\left(\geq 2\right)
is fixed and
m
is fixed. Then

E\left|\mathbf{\beta}_{j0}^{\prime}\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{w}\bar{w}}\mathbf{\beta}_{j0}\right|=O\left(n^{-1/2}T^{-2}\right)\text{, for }j=1,2,...,r_{0}\text{,}

(A.48)

where
\mathbf{\beta}_{j0}
are eigenvectors of
\left(6q\right)^{-1}\left(q-1\right)\mathbf{\Psi}_{n}
defined in Assumption
3
.

Proof.
Premultiplying (
20
) by
\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}
, postmultiplying
by
\mathbf{B}_{0}
, and noting
\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{C}_{i}=\mathbf{0}
for
i=1,2,...,n
under Assumption
3
, we obtain

\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{w}\bar{w}}\mathbf{B}_{0}=\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{v}\bar{v}}\mathbf{B}_{0}\text{,}

where by the orthonormality requirement
\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{B}_{0}=\mathbf{I}_{r_{0}}
. Using Lemma
A.5
, there exists a positive
constant
K
such that for any
j=1,2,...,r
,

E\left(\mathbf{\beta}_{j0}\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{v}\bar{v}}\mathbf{\beta}_{j0}\right)^{2}<Kn^{-1}T^{-4}\text{.}

Result (
A.48
) directly follows.

Lemma A.7

Consider
\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{w}\bar{w}}=T^{-1}n^{-1}q^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}\left(\mathbf{\bar{w}}_{i\ell}-\mathbf{\bar{w}}_{i\circ}\right)\left(\mathbf{\bar{w}}_{i\ell}-\mathbf{\bar{w}}_{i\circ}\right)^{\prime}
and the associated
m\times r
matrix
\mathbf{\hat{B}}_{0}
given by orthonormal eigenvectors of
\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{w}\bar{w}}
corresponding to its
r_{0}
smallest eigenvalues collected in the
r_{0}\times r_{0}
diagonal matrix
\mathbf{\hat{\Lambda}}_{0}=diag\left(\hat{\lambda}_{1},\hat{\lambda}_{2},...,\hat{\lambda}_{r_{0}}\right)
.
Suppose the
m\times 1
vector
\mathbf{w}_{it}
is given by (
1
)
without interactive time effects (
\mathbf{G}_{i}=\mathbf{0}
), Assumptions
1
to
3
hold,
m
and
q

\left(\geq 2\right)
are fixed
integers. Then

E\left\|\mathbf{\hat{\Lambda}}_{0}\right\|=O\left(n^{-1/2}T^{-2}\right)\text{.}

(A.49)

\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{w}\bar{w}}\mathbf{\hat{B}}_{0}=O_{p}\left(n^{-1/2}T^{-2}\right)\text{.}

(A.50)

Proof.
We have
\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{w}\bar{w}}\mathbf{\hat{B}}_{0}=\mathbf{\mathbf{\hat{B}}}_{0}\mathbf{\hat{\Lambda}}_{0}
,

where
\mathbf{\hat{\Lambda}}_{0}=diag\left(\hat{\lambda}_{1},\hat{\lambda}_{2},...,\hat{\lambda}_{r_{0}}\right)
is consisting of
r_{0}
smallest eigenvalues of
\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{w}\bar{w}}
, denoted as
0\leq\hat{\lambda}_{1}\leq\hat{\lambda}_{2}\leq...\leq\hat{\lambda}_{r_{0}}
, and
\mathbf{\hat{B}}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{\hat{B}}_{0}=\mathbf{I}_{r}
. Noting
\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{B}_{0}=\mathbf{I}_{r}
then

0\leq tr\left(\mathbf{\hat{\Lambda}}_{0}\right)=tr\left(\mathbf{\hat{B}}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{w}\bar{w}}\mathbf{\hat{B}}_{0}\right)\leq tr\left(\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{w}\bar{w}}\mathbf{B}_{0}\right)\text{.}

But result (
A.48
) of Lemma
A.6
implies
E\left[tr\left(\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{w}\bar{w}}\mathbf{B}_{0}\right)\right]=O\left(n^{-1/2}T^{-2}\right)
and it follows that
E\left\|\mathbf{\hat{\Lambda}}_{0}\right\|=O\left(n^{-1/2}T^{-2}\right)
, as
required. Taking norm of
\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{w}\bar{w}}\mathbf{\hat{B}}_{0}=\mathbf{\hat{B}}_{0}\mathbf{\hat{\Lambda}}_{0}
, and noting that
\left\|\mathbf{\hat{B}}_{0}\right\|=1
, we obtain
\left\|\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{w}\bar{w}}\mathbf{\hat{B}}_{0}\right\|\leq\left\|\mathbf{\hat{\Lambda}}_{0}\right\|
. Taking expectations and using (
A.49
) yields
E\left\|\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{w}\bar{w}}\mathbf{\hat{B}}_{0}\right\|=O\left(n^{-1/2}T^{-2}\right)
, and result (
A.50
) follows.

Lemma A.8

Suppose Assumptions
1
to
3
hold,
q\left(\geq 2\right)
is fixed and
m
is fixed. Consider
\mathbf{\bar{\xi}}_{iq}=q^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}\left(\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}\mathbf{\otimes\bar{s}}_{i\ell}\right)-\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}\mathbf{\otimes\bar{s}}_{i\circ}
, where
\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\ell}
,
\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\circ}
are the sub-sample and full sample time averages of the partial
sum process
\mathbf{s}_{it}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{t}\mathbf{u}_{it}
, and
\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}
,
\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}
are the sub-sample
and full sample time averages of
\mathbf{v}_{it}=\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\ast}(L)\mathbf{u}_{it}=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\mathbf{C}_{ij}^{\ast}\mathbf{u}_{i,t-j}
. Let
\mathbf{\Omega}_{\xi_{qi}}=Var\left(\mathbf{\bar{\xi}}_{iq}\right)
and suppose
\mathbf{\Omega}_{q}=\lim_{n,T\rightarrow\infty}\left(\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{\otimes\mathbf{C}}_{i}\right)\mathbf{\Omega}_{\xi_{qi}}\left(\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}\mathbf{\otimes\mathbf{C}}_{i}^{\prime}\right)
is positive definite. Then

n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{\otimes\mathbf{C}}_{i}\right)\mathbf{\tilde{\xi}}_{iq}\rightarrow_{d}N\left(\mathbf{0,\Omega}_{q}\right),

(A.51)

for
n,T\rightarrow\infty\,
jointly (in no particular order), where
\mathbf{\tilde{\xi}}_{iq}=\mathbf{\bar{\xi}}_{iq}-E\left(\mathbf{\bar{\xi}}_{iq}\right)
.

Proof.
Let
n_{T}=n\left(T\right)
be any non-decreasing integer-valued function
of
T
such that
\lim_{T\rightarrow\infty}n_{T}=\infty
, and let
\mathbf{\omega}
be any
m^{2}\times 1
vector of unit length, namely
\left\|\mathbf{\omega}\right\|=1
. Define the following triangular
array

a_{n_{T},i}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{T}}}\mathbf{\omega}^{\prime}\mathbf{\Omega}_{q}^{-1/2}\left(\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{\otimes\mathbf{C}}_{i}\right)\left[\mathbf{\bar{\xi}}_{iq}-E\left(\mathbf{\bar{\xi}}_{iq}\right)\right]\text{,}

where
m^{2}\times 1
vector
\mathbf{\bar{\xi}}_{iq}
depends on
T
, but
this subscript is suppressed for notational simplicity. By construction
array
\left\{a_{n_{T},i}\right\}
is zero mean. By assumption
1
,

\mathbf{u}_{it}
is independently distributed over
i
and
t
, and
therefore
a_{n_{T},i}
is independent of
a_{n_{T},j}
for
i\neq j
. In
addition,

\sum_{i=1}^{n_{T}}Var\left(a_{n_{T},i}\right)=1\text{.}

We establish next the Liapunov’s condition, given by

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{T}}E\left|a_{n_{T},i}\right|^{2+\epsilon}=0\text{ for some }\epsilon>0\text{,}

(A.52)

is met. Since
\mathbf{\Omega}_{\xi_{q}}
is positive definite then
\left\|\mathbf{\omega}^{\prime}\mathbf{\Omega}_{\xi_{q}}^{-1/2}\left(\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{\otimes\mathbf{C}}_{i}\right)\right\|<K
, and (
A.52
) will hold if

\sup_{i,k,T}E\left|\tilde{\xi}_{iqk}\right|^{2+\epsilon}<K\text{
for some }\epsilon>0\text{,}

(A.53)

where
\tilde{\xi}_{iqk}
is
k
-th element of
\mathbf{\tilde{\xi}}_{iq}
.
Lemma
A.4
establish (
A.53
) holds, and therefore (
A.52
) is
met, as required. Then, by Liapunov’s theorem (Theorem 23.11 in

?
)), the Lindeberg condition is also met, and (
A.51
) follows by Theorem
23.6 in

?
).

Lemma A.9

Consider
m\times m
matrices
\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{s}_{i}\bar{v}_{i}}=T^{-1}q^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}\left(\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\ell}-\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\circ}\right)\left(\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}-\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}\right)^{\prime}
, where
\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\ell}
,
\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\circ}
are the sub-sample and full sample time averages of the
partial sum process
\mathbf{s}_{it}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{t}\mathbf{u}_{it}
, and

\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}
,
\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}
are the sub-sample
and full sample time averages of
\mathbf{v}_{it}=\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\ast}(L)\mathbf{u}_{it}=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\mathbf{C}_{ij}^{\ast}\mathbf{u}_{i,t-j}
. Suppose Assumptions
1
and
2
hold,
q
(
\geq 2
) is fixed and
m
is fixed. Then

n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{C}_{i}\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{s}_{i}\bar{v}_{i}}=O_{p}\left(n^{-1/2}T^{-1}\right)+O\left(T^{-2}\right)

(A.54)

Proof.
For the purpose of this proof, define
m^{2}\times 1
vector
\mathbf{b}_{nT}
obtained by vectorization of
n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{C}_{i}\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{s}_{i}\bar{v}_{i}}
,

\mathbf{b}_{nT}=vec\left(n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{C}_{i}\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{s}_{i}\bar{v}_{i}}\right)=T^{-1}n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\mathbf{I}_{m}\otimes\mathbf{C}_{i}\right)\mathbf{\bar{\xi}}_{iq}\text{,}

where
\mathbf{\bar{\xi}}_{iq}=q^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}\left(\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}\mathbf{\otimes\bar{s}}_{i\ell}\right)-\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}\mathbf{\otimes\bar{s}}_{i\circ}
. Denote the
k
-
th
element of
\mathbf{b}_{nT}
as
b_{k,nT}=\mathbf{e}_{k}^{\prime}\mathbf{b}_{nT}
,
where
\mathbf{e}_{k}
is
m^{2}\times 1
selection vector for the
k
-th
element,
k=1,2,...,m^{2}
. Define
\tilde{b}_{k,nT}=b_{k,nT}-E\left(b_{k,nT}\right)
. Using result (
A.12
) of Lemma
A.3
and noting
\sup_{i}\left\|\mathbf{C}_{i}\right\|<K
by Assumption
2
,
we obtain

\sup_{k}E\left(b_{k,nT}\right)=O\left(T^{-2}\right)\text{.}

(A.55)

Consider
E\left(\tilde{b}_{k,nT}\right)^{2}
next. We have

E\left(\tilde{b}_{k,nT}\right)^{2}=E\left\{\left[T^{-1}n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{e}_{k}^{\prime}\left(I_{m}\otimes\mathbf{C}_{i}\right)\mathbf{\tilde{\xi}}_{iq}\right]^{2}\right\}\text{,}

where
\mathbf{\tilde{\xi}}_{iq}=\mathbf{\bar{\xi}}_{iq}-E\left(\mathbf{\bar{\xi}}_{iq}\right)
. By construction
E\left(\mathbf{\tilde{\xi}}_{iq}\right)=0
. In addition,
\mathbf{\tilde{\xi}}_{iq}
is independent of

\mathbf{\tilde{\xi}}_{jq}
for any
i\neq j
. It follows

\displaystyle E\left(\tilde{b}_{k,nT}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n^{-2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}E\left(\mathbf{e}_{k}^{\prime}\left(\mathbf{I}_{m}\otimes\mathbf{C}_{i}\right)\mathbf{\tilde{\xi}}_{iq}\right)^{2}\text{,}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle T^{-2}n^{-2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|\mathbf{e}_{k}^{\prime}\left(\mathbf{I}_{m}\otimes\mathbf{C}_{i}\right)\right\|^{2}E\left(\left\|\mathbf{\tilde{\xi}}_{iq}\right\|^{2}\right)\text{.}

Using result (
A.38
) of Lemma
A.4
and
\sup_{i}\left\|\mathbf{C}_{i}\right\|<K
(by Assumption
2
), then

\sup_{k}E\left(\tilde{b}_{k,nT}\right)^{2}=O\left(n^{-1}T^{-2}\right)\text{.}

(A.56)

Using (
A.55
) and (
A.56
) in
b_{k,nT}=\tilde{b}_{k,nT}+E\left(b_{k,nT}\right)
, we obtain (
A.54
).

A.2
Proofs of Propositions and Theorems

Proof of Proposition
2
.

Under Assumption
3

\left(n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{C}_{i}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\prime}\right)\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}\mathbf{=\Psi}_{n}\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}\mathbf{=0,}
for any
n
and as

n\rightarrow\infty
. Then we have
\mathbf{\Psi\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}=0=}\left(\mathbf{P}^{\prime}\mathbf{P}\right)\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}\mathbf{=0}
. But by condition
rank\left(\mathbf{\Psi}\right)=m-r

of Assumption
3
,
\mathbf{P}
is an
(m-r)\times m\,
full row rank
matrix. Then
\mathbf{PP}^{\prime}
is non-singular, and we must also have
\mathbf{P\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}=0}
. Combing this result with (
31
) now yields

\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\mathbf{R}\\
\mathbf{P}\end{array}\right)\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}\mathbf{=}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\mathbf{A}\\
\mathbf{0}\end{array}\right).

(A.57)

Under Assumptions
3
and the exact
r^{2}
restrictions given by (
31
) with
rank\left(\mathbf{A}\right)=rank\left(\mathbf{R}\right)=r<m
, condition

rank\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\mathbf{P}_{n}\text{,}\\
\mathbf{R}\end{array}\right)=rank\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\mathbf{P}\\
\mathbf{R}\end{array}\right)=m

holds,
\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\mathbf{R+P}^{\prime}\mathbf{P}
is a positive
definite matrix, and
\mathbf{\beta\,}
is uniquely determined by

\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}=\left(\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\mathbf{R+P}^{\prime}\mathbf{P}\right)^{-1}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\mathbf{A}\\
\mathbf{0}\end{array}\right)=\left(\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\mathbf{R+\Psi}\right)^{-1}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\mathbf{A}\\
\mathbf{0}\end{array}\right)\text{.}

Now using the normalizations
\mathbf{R=(I}_{r},\mathbf{0})^{\prime}
and
\mathbf{A=I}_{r}
and conformably partitioning
\mathbf{\Psi}
we have

\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\mathbf{R+\Psi=}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathbf{I}_{r}+\mathbf{\Psi}_{11}&\mathbf{\Psi}_{21}^{\prime}\\
\mathbf{\Psi}_{21}&\mathbf{\Psi}_{22}\end{array}\right).

Since
\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\mathbf{R+\Psi}
is a positive definite matrix
then the
r\times r
and
(m-r)\times\left(m-r\right)
matrices
\mathbf{I}_{r}+\mathbf{\Psi}_{11}
and
\mathbf{\Psi}_{22}
are also positive
definite. Solving the following system of equations now yields

\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathbf{I}_{r}+\mathbf{\Psi}_{11}&\mathbf{\Psi}_{21}^{\prime}\\
\mathbf{\Psi}_{21}&\mathbf{\Psi}_{22}\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0,1}\\
\mathbf{\Theta}\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\mathbf{I}_{r}\\
\mathbf{0}\end{array}\right),

and we have

\displaystyle\left(\mathbf{I}_{r}+\mathbf{\Psi}_{11}\right)\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0,1}+\mathbf{\Psi}_{21}^{\prime}\mathbf{\Theta}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}_{r},

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Psi}_{21}\mathbf{\beta}_{1}+\mathbf{\Psi}_{22}\mathbf{\Theta=0.}

Since
\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0,1}=\mathbf{I}_{r}
it follows that

\left(\mathbf{I}_{r}+\mathbf{\Psi}_{11}\right)+\mathbf{\Psi}_{21}^{\prime}\mathbf{\Theta}=\mathbf{I}_{r},\text{ and }\mathbf{\Theta=-\Psi}_{22}^{-1}\mathbf{\Psi}_{21},

which implies that
\mathbf{\Psi}_{11}=\mathbf{\Psi}_{21}^{\prime}\mathbf{\Psi}_{22}^{-1}\mathbf{\Psi}_{21}
(and ensures that
\mathbf{\Psi}
is
rank deficient, as required under Assumption
3
).

Proof of Proposition
1
.

Consider the individual terms on the right side of (
20
), which, for
convenience, we copy below:

\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{w}\bar{w}}=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{C}_{i}\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{s}_{i}\bar{s}_{i}}\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\prime}+n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{C}_{i}\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{s}_{i}\bar{v}_{i}}+n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{v}_{i}\bar{s}_{i}}^{\prime}\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\prime}+n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{v}_{i}\bar{v}_{i}}.

Taking norm and expectations of the second term, we obtain

E\left\|n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{C}_{i}\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{s}_{i}\bar{v}_{i}}\right\|\leq n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|\mathbf{C}_{i}\right\|E\left\|\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{s}_{i}\bar{v}_{i}}\right\|\leq\sup_{i}\left\|\mathbf{C}_{i}\right\|\sup_{i}E\left\|\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{s}_{i}\bar{v}_{i}}\right\|=O\left(T^{-1}\right)\text{,}

where Assumption
2
ensures
\sup_{i}\left\|\mathbf{C}_{i}\right\|<K
and result (
A.11
) of Lemma
A.3
establishes
\sup_{i}E\left\|\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{s}_{i}\bar{v}_{i}}\right\|=O\left(T^{-1}\right)
. Similarly,

E\left\|n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{v}_{i}\bar{s}_{i}}^{\prime}\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\prime}\right\|=O\left(T^{-1}\right)\text{,}

and, using result (
A.9
) of Lemma
A.3
,

E\left\|n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{v}_{i}\bar{v}_{i}}\right\|=O\left(T^{-2}\right)\text{.}

Using these results in (
20
) yields

E\left\|\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{w}\bar{w}}-n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{C}_{i}\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{s}_{i}\bar{s}_{i}}\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\prime}\right\|=O\left(T^{-1}\right)\text{.}

(A.58)

Consider the term
n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{C}_{i}\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{s}_{i}\bar{s}_{i}}\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\prime}
next. We can write this term as

n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{C}_{i}\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{s}_{i}\bar{s}_{i}}\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\prime}=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{C}_{i}E\left(\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{s}_{i}\bar{s}_{i}}\right)\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\prime}+n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{V}_{i}\text{,}

(A.59)

where
\mathbf{V}_{i}=\mathbf{C}_{i}\left[\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{s}_{i}\bar{s}_{i}}-E\left(\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{s}_{i}\bar{s}_{i}}\right)\right]\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\prime}
. Using result (
A.3
) of Lemma
A.2
and noting
that
sup_{i}\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\right\|<K
under Assumption
1
, we obtain

n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{C}_{i}E\left(\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{s}_{i}\bar{s}_{i}}\right)\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\prime}=\frac{(q-1)}{6q}\mathbf{\Psi}_{n}+O\left(T^{-2}\right)\text{,}

(A.60)

where
\mathbf{\Psi}_{n}=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{C}_{i}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{i}\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\prime}
. In addition, uniformly bounded fourth moments
of individual elements of
\mathbf{u}_{it}
ensure variances of individual
elements
\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{s}_{i}\bar{s}_{i}}
are bounded. Since also
\sup_{i}\left\|\mathbf{C}_{i}\right\|<K
by Assumption
2
,
it follows variances of the individual elements of
\mathbf{V}_{i}
are
uniformly bounded. Noting that
E(\mathbf{V}_{i})=\mathbf{0}
by
construction, and that
\mathbf{V}_{i}
is independently distributed of
\mathbf{V}_{j}
for all
i\neq j
(by Assumption
1
), it follows

n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{V}_{i}=O_{p}\left(n^{-1/2}\right)\text{.}

(A.61)

Using (
A.60
) and (
A.61
) in (
A.59
) yields

n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{C}_{i}\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{s}_{i}\bar{s}_{i}}\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\prime}=\frac{(q-1)}{6q}\mathbf{\Psi}_{n}+O_{p}\left(n^{-1/2}\right)+O\left(T^{-2}\right)\text{,}

(A.62)

and using this result in (

), we obtain

\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{w}\bar{w}}\rightarrow_{p}\frac{(q-1)}{6q}\mathbf{\Psi}\text{,}

as

(in no particular order), where

. This completes the proof of

for all

\mathbf{\Psi}_{n}\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}\mathbf{=\mathbf{\Psi}\mathring{B}}_{0}=\mathbf{0}
.

(A.64)

Lemma

established

(A.65)

Subtracting (

\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{w}\bar{w}}\left(\widehat{\mathbf{\mathring{B}}}_{0}-\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}\right)=-\left(n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{C}_{i}\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{s}_{i}\bar{v}_{i}}\right)\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}-\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{v}\bar{v}}\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}+O_{p}\left(n^{-1/2}T^{-2}\right)\mathbf{.}

(A.66)

Result (
A.47
) of Lemma (
A.5
) implies
\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{v}\bar{v}}=O_{p}\left(n^{-1/2}T^{-2}\right)
, and we have

\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{w}\bar{w}}\left(\widehat{\mathbf{\mathring{B}}}_{0}-\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}\right)=-\left(n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{C}_{i}\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{s}_{i}\bar{v}_{i}}\right)\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}+O_{p}\left(n^{-1/2}T^{-2}\right)\mathbf{.}

Multiplying above equation by
\sqrt{n}T
we obtain,

\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{w}\bar{w}}\sqrt{n}T\left(\widehat{\mathbf{\mathring{B}}}_{0}-\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}\right)=-\left(n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}T\mathbf{C}_{i}\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{s}_{i}\bar{v}_{i}}\right)\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}+O_{p}\left(T^{-1}\right).

(A.67)

The first term on the right side of (
A.67
) can be written as

\left(n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}T\mathbf{C}_{i}\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{s}_{i}\bar{v}_{i}}\right)\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}=\left(n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{Z}_{i}\right)\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}+\frac{\sqrt{n}}{T}\left[n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{C}_{i}E\left(T^{2}\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{s}_{i}\bar{v}_{i}}\right)\right]\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}\text{,}

where
\mathbf{Z}_{i}=\mathbf{C}_{i}\left[T\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{s}_{i}\bar{v}_{i}}-E\left(T\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{s}_{i}\bar{v}_{i}}\right)\right]\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}
. Using result (
A.12
) of Lemma
A.3
, we obtain

\left\|n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{C}_{i}E\left(T^{2}\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{s}_{i}\bar{v}_{i}}\right)\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}\right\|\leq T^{2}\sup_{i}\left\|\mathbf{C}_{i}\right\|\sup_{i}\left\|E\left(\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{s}_{i}\bar{v}_{i}}\right)\right\|\left\|\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}\right\|<K\text{,}

(A.68)

where
\left\|\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}\right\|<K
and
\sup_{i}\left\|\mathbf{C}_{i}\right\|_{1}<K
by Assumption
2
. Using the above result in (
A.67
) it follows that

\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{w}\bar{w}}\sqrt{n}T\left(\widehat{\mathbf{\mathring{B}}}_{0}-\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}\right)=-n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{Z}_{i}+O_{p}\left(\frac{\sqrt{n}}{T}\right)+O_{p}\left(T^{-1}\right),

Vectorizing the above equation we have

\left(\mathbf{I}_{r}\mathbf{\otimes Q}_{\bar{w}\bar{w}}\right)\sqrt{n}T\text{ $vec$}\left(\widehat{\mathbf{\mathring{B}}}_{0}-\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}\right)=n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{\otimes\mathbf{C}}_{i}\right)\mathbf{\tilde{\xi}}_{iq}+O_{p}\left(\frac{\sqrt{n}}{T}\right)+O_{p}\left(T^{-1}\right)\text{,}

(A.69)

where
\mathbf{\tilde{\xi}}_{iq}=\mathbf{\bar{\xi}}_{iq}-E\left(\mathbf{\bar{\xi}}_{iq}\right)
, and
\mathbf{\bar{\xi}}_{iq}
is given by (recall
\mathbf{Z}_{i}=\mathbf{C}_{i}\left[T\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{s}_{i}\bar{v}_{i}}-E\left(T\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{s}_{i}\bar{v}_{i}}\right)\right]\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}
and
\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{s}_{i}\bar{v}_{i}}=T^{-1}q^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}\left(\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\ell}-\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\circ}\right)\left(\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}-\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}\right)^{\prime}
)

\displaystyle\mathbf{\bar{\xi}}_{iq}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle q^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}vec\left[\left(\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\ell}-\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\circ}\right)\left(\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}-\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}\right)^{\prime}\right]=q^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}\left(\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}-\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}\right)\mathbf{\otimes}\left(\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\ell}-\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\circ}\right)\text{,}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle q^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}\mathbf{\otimes\bar{s}}_{i\ell}-\left(q^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}\right)\mathbf{\otimes\bar{s}}_{i\circ}-\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}\mathbf{\otimes}\left(q^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\ell}\right)+\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}\mathbf{\otimes\bar{s}}_{i\circ}\text{.}

Using
q^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}=\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}
and
q^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\ell}=\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\circ}
, the expression for
\mathbf{\bar{\xi}}_{iq}
above
simplifies to

\mathbf{\bar{\xi}}_{iq}=q^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}\left(\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}-\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}\right)\mathbf{\otimes}\left(\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\ell}-\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\circ}\right)=q^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}\left(\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}\mathbf{\otimes\bar{s}}_{i\ell}\right)-\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}\mathbf{\otimes\bar{s}}_{i\circ}\text{.}

Lemma
A.8
established convergence in distribution for
n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\mathbf{\mathring{B}}^{\prime}\mathbf{\otimes\mathbf{C}}_{i}\right)\mathbf{\tilde{\xi}}_{iq}
. Using this Lemma in (
A.69
), and noting that
d>1/2
implies
\sqrt{n}/T\rightarrow 0
as
n,T\rightarrow\infty
, we obtain (
41
), as required.

This online theory supplement describes implementation of the PME
estimator for unbalanced panels (Section
TS-1
), and provides theorems
and proofs for the consistency and asymptotic distribution of the PME
estimator for the model with interactive time effects (Section
TS-2
).

Appendix TS-1
PME estimator for unbalanced panels

We assume there are
n
cross section units,
i=1,2,...,n
, with each cross
section unit having
T_{i}
consecutive observations. We do not allow for
gaps. Let
T_{m}=\max T_{i}
. We also assume all
T_{i}
’s expand at the
same rate with
n
. Specifically, there exists a positive constant
\varkappa_{u}>0
, which does not depend on the sample size, such that
T_{i}>\varkappa_{u}\cdot T_{m}
, for
i=1,2....,n
.

In practice, these assumption may require excluding some observations to
avoid gaps, and excluding individual cross section units where
T_{i}
is
very small compared with the rest of the panel (see Subsection
TS-1.1
). In
the exposition below, we assume that the unbalanced panel satisfies the
assumptions above.

To accommodate unbalanced panels, we consider the following generalization
of sub-sample time averages defined in Section
4
(see (
8
)),

\mathbf{\bar{w}}_{i\ell}=\frac{1}{T_{i\ell}}\sum_{t=H_{i,\ell-1}+1}^{H_{i\ell}}\mathbf{w}_{it},\text{ for }\ell=1,2,...,q_{i}\text{,}

(TS.1)

where
T_{i\ell}=H_{i\ell}-H_{i,\ell-1}
is the sample size for computing
sub-sample time average
\ell
for unit
i
. We continue to assume
H_{i\ell}>H_{i,\ell-1}
,
T_{i}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{q_{i}}T_{i\ell}
, and we
assume
T_{i\ell}
is of same order as
T_{i}
, namely there exists
positive constants
K>0
that do not depend on the sample size such that
T_{i\ell}/\max_{\ell=1,2,...,q_{i}}\left\{T_{i\ell}\right\}>K_{0}
for
all
i
and all
\ell
. The number of sub-sample time averages,
q_{i}
, in
(
TS.1
) are allowed to vary across units with
2\leq q_{i}\leq q_{m}
,
where
q_{m}
does not depend on the sample size. The time average
\mathbf{\bar{w}}_{i\circ}
is given by

\mathbf{\bar{w}}_{i\circ}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}\tilde{q}_{i\ell}\mathbf{\bar{w}}_{i\ell}\text{,}

and we define

\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{w}_{i}\bar{w}_{i}}=T_{i}^{-1}q_{i}^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q_{i}}\left(\mathbf{\bar{w}}_{i\ell}-\mathbf{\bar{w}}_{i\circ}\right)\left(\mathbf{\bar{w}}_{i\ell}-\mathbf{\bar{w}}_{i\circ}\right)^{\prime}\text{,}

(TS.2)

and

\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{w}\bar{w}}=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{w}_{i}\bar{w}_{i}}=T_{ave}^{-1}n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\phi_{i}q_{i}^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q_{i}}\left(\mathbf{\bar{w}}_{i\ell}-\mathbf{\bar{w}}_{i\circ}\right)\left(\mathbf{\bar{w}}_{i\ell}-\mathbf{\bar{w}}_{i\circ}\right)^{\prime}\right)\text{,}

where

\phi_{i}=\left(\frac{T_{i}}{T_{ave}}\right)^{-1}\text{ and }T_{ave}=\left(n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}T_{i}^{-1}\right)^{-1}\text{.}

Following the same arguments as in the case of balanced panels, it can be
established that
E\left\|\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{v}_{i}\bar{v}_{i}}\right\|_{1}<K/T_{i}^{2}
,
E\left\|\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{s}_{i}\bar{v}_{i}}\right\|_{1}<K/T_{i}
, and
E\left\|\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{s}_{i}\bar{s}_{i}}\right\|<K
. These results imply
\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{w}\bar{w}}\mathbf{\beta}=O_{p}\left(T_{ave}^{-1}\right)
and we can use the
eigenvectors corresponding to the
r_{0}
smallest eigenvalues of
\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{w}\bar{w}}
to estimate cointegrating relationships subject to the
exact identifying assumptions.

To derive asymptotic distribution, let

\mathbf{Z}_{i}=\mathbf{C}_{i}\left[\phi_{i}q_{i}^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q_{i}}\left(\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\ell}-\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\circ}\right)\left(\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}-\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}\right)^{\prime}\right]\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}-\mathbf{C}_{i}\left[\phi_{i}q_{i}^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q_{i}}E\left(\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\ell}-\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\circ}\right)\left(\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}-\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}\right)^{\prime}\right]\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}\text{,}

(TS.3)

and following the same arguments as in Section
4
, we obtain

\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{w}\bar{w}}\sqrt{n}T_{ave}\left(\widehat{\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}}-\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}\right)=-n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{Z}_{i}+O_{p}\left(\frac{\sqrt{n}}{T_{ave}}\right),

(TS.4)

Consider the exact identifying restrictions
\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}=\left(\mathbf{I}_{r_{0}},\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0,2}^{\prime}\right)^{\prime}
, where
\mathbf{I}_{r_{0}}
is an identity matrix of order
r_{0}
. Let
\mathbf{\hat{\Theta}}
and
\mathbf{\Theta}_{0}
be the lower
\left(m-r_{0}\right)\times r_{0}
block of
\widehat{\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}}
and
\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}
, respectively. Then,
\sqrt{n}T_{ave}vec\left(\mathbf{\hat{\Theta}}-\mathbf{\Theta}_{0}\right)

converges to a normal distribution and the variance of
\mathbf{\hat{\Theta}}
can be estimated as

\widehat{Var}\left(\mathbf{\hat{\Theta}}\right)=\frac{1}{nT_{ave}^{2}}\mathbf{Q}_{22,\bar{w}\bar{w}}^{-1}\mathbf{\hat{\Omega}}_{z,22}\mathbf{Q}_{22,\bar{w}\bar{w}}^{-1}\text{,}

(TS.5)

where

\mathbf{\hat{\Omega}}_{z}=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\phi_{i}^{2}q_{i}^{-2}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q_{i}}\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=1}^{q_{i}}\left(\mathbf{\bar{E}}_{i\ell}\mathbf{\otimes I}_{m}\right)\left(\mathbf{\bar{w}}_{i\ell}-\mathbf{\bar{w}}_{i\circ}\right)\left(\mathbf{\bar{w}}_{i\ell^{\prime}}-\mathbf{\bar{w}}_{i\circ}\right)^{\prime}\left(\mathbf{\mathbf{\bar{E}}}_{i\ell^{\prime}}^{\prime}\mathbf{\otimes I}_{m}\right).

(TS.6)

and, as before,
\mathbf{Q}_{22,\bar{w}\bar{w}}
and
\mathbf{\hat{\Omega}}_{z,22}
are the
\left(m-r_{0}\right)\times\left(m-r_{0}\right)
lower
blocks of matrices
\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{w}\bar{w}}
and
\mathbf{\hat{\Omega}}_{z}
, respectively.

TS-1.1
Implementation of unbalanced PME estimator in empirical
application

Let
T_{m}=\max T_{i}
. In empirical application in Section
10
we
exclude cross section units with gaps in data and we exclude units with
T_{i}<20
years. The remaining units are used for estimation, and we denote
the number of these units as
n
.

Definition of sub-sample sample time averages in (
TS.1
) is quite
general, and allows for a variety of asymptotically justified options to
implement the PME estimator for unbalanced panels. In view of inference in
small samples being adversely affected by a larger choices of
q
, we set
q_{i}=2
for all
i
, and we divide the
T_{i}
consecutive observations
into two halves when
T_{i}
is even. If
T_{i}
is odd, we compute the
first sub-sample time average based on
\left(T_{i}+1\right)/2

observations, and the second sub-sample time average based on the remaining
\left(T_{i}-1\right)/2
observations. This version of unbalanced PME
estimator is considered in the empirical application.

Appendix TS-2
Consistency and asymptotic distribution of PME estimator in the
model with interactive time effects

Theorems
3
-
4
extends Theorems
1
-
2
to
model with interactive time effects.

Theorem 3

Consider the panel data model for the
m\times 1
vector
\mathbf{w}_{it}
given by (

) and suppose that Assumptions
1

to

hold, and the number of cointegrating vectors,

,

and

jointly such that

, for some

.

.

Using (

Multiplying this expression by

from the

from the right, and noting eigenvectors

\mathbf{\hat{B}}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{w}\bar{w}}\mathbf{\hat{B}}_{0}=\frac{(q-1)}{6q}\mathbf{\hat{B}}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{\Psi}_{n}\mathbf{\hat{B}}_{0}+O_{p}\left(n^{-1/2}\right)+O_{p}\left(T^{-1}\right)\text{,}

(TS.1)

where

as

. (

.

Theorem 4

Consider the panel data model for the

\mathbf{\Omega}_{q}^{\ast}=lim_{n,T\rightarrow\infty}\left[n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{\otimes\mathbf{C}}_{i}\right)\mathbf{\Omega}_{\xi_{qi}^{\ast}}\left(\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}\mathbf{\otimes\mathbf{C}}_{i}^{\prime}\right)\right],

4
.

Averaging (

) over

\displaystyle\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{w}\bar{w}}

\displaystyle=

(TS.4)

\displaystyle+n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{v}_{i}\bar{s}_{i}}^{\prime}\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\prime}+n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{f}_{i}\bar{s}_{i}}^{\prime}\mathbf{C}_{i}+\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{f}\bar{f}}+\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{f}\bar{v}}+\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{v}\bar{f}}+\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{v}\bar{v}}\text{.}

Multiplying both sides of (
TS.4
) by
\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}
from
the right, and noting that
\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\prime}\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}=\mathbf{0}
under Assumption
3
, we have

\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{w}\bar{w}}\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}=\left(n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{C}_{i}\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{s}_{i}\bar{v}_{i}}\right)\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}+\left(n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{C}_{i}\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{s}_{i}\bar{f}_{i}}\right)\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}+\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{f}\bar{f}}\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}+\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{f}\bar{v}}\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}+\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{v}\bar{f}}\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}+\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{v}\bar{v}}\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}\text{,}

(TS.5)

where by results (

)-(

) of Lemma
