,
\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{f}\bar{f}}=O_{p}\left(T^{-2}\right)
,
\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{f}\bar{v}}=\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{v}\bar{f}}^{\prime}=O_{p}\left(T^{-2}\right)
, and by
result (
A.47
) of Lemma (
A.5
)
\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{v}\bar{v}}=O_{p}\left(n^{-1/2}T^{-2}\right)
. Using result (
TS.18
) of Lemma
TS-2.3
, we have
\left\|\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{w}\bar{w}}\mathbf{\hat{B}}_{0}\right\|=O_{p}\left(T^{-2}\right)
, and given that
\widehat{\mathbf{\mathring{B}}}_{0}
is an
O_{p}\left(1\right)
rotation of
\mathbf{\hat{B}}_{0}
, it follows

\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{w}\bar{w}}\widehat{\mathbf{\mathring{B}}}_{0}=O_{p}\left(T^{-2}\right)\text{.}

(TS.6)

Subtracting (
TS.5
) from (
TS.6
) yields (noting
O_{p}\left(T^{-2}\right)
dominates
O_{p}\left(n^{-1/2}T^{-2}\right)
)

\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{w}\bar{w}}\left(\widehat{\mathbf{\mathring{B}}}-\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}\right)=-\left(n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{C}_{i}\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{s}_{i}\bar{v}_{i}}\right)\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}+\left(n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{C}_{i}\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{s}_{i}\bar{f}_{i}}\right)\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}+O_{p}\left(T^{-2}\right)\mathbf{.}

Defining
\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{s}_{i}\bar{\omega}_{i}}\mathbf{=Q}_{\bar{s}_{i}\bar{v}_{i}}+\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{s}_{i}\bar{f}_{i}}
and multiplying above
equation by
\sqrt{n}T
we obtain,

\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{w}\bar{w}}\sqrt{n}T\left(\widehat{\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}}-\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}\right)=-\left(n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}T\mathbf{C}_{i}\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{s}_{i}\bar{\omega}_{i}}\right)\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}+O_{p}\left(\frac{\sqrt{n}}{T}\right)\mathbf{.}

(TS.7)

Noting
\mathbf{s}_{it}
is independently distributed of
\mathbf{f}_{it^{\prime}}
for all
t,t^{\prime}
, the first term on the right side
of (
TS.7
) can be written as

\left(n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}T\mathbf{C}_{i}\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{s}_{i}\bar{\omega}_{i}}\right)\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}=\left(n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{Z}_{i}^{\ast}\right)\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}+\frac{\sqrt{n}}{T}\left[n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{C}_{i}E\left(T^{2}\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{s}_{i}\bar{v}_{i}}\right)\right]\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}\text{,}

where
\mathbf{Z}_{i}^{\ast}=\mathbf{C}_{i}\left[T\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{s}_{i}\bar{\omega}_{i}}-E\left(T\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{s}_{i}\bar{\omega}_{i}}\right)\right]\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}
, and
E\left(T^{2}\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{s}_{i}\bar{\omega}_{i}}\right)=E\left(T^{2}\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{s}_{i}\bar{v}_{i}}\right)
. Using (
A.68
), it follows that

\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{w}\bar{w}}\sqrt{n}T\left(\widehat{\mathbf{\mathring{B}}}_{0}-\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}\right)=-n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{Z}_{i}^{\ast}+O_{p}\left(\frac{\sqrt{n}}{T}\right),

Vectorizing the above equation we have

\left(\mathbf{I}_{r}\mathbf{\otimes Q}_{\bar{w}\bar{w}}\right)\sqrt{n}T\text{ $vec$}\left(\widehat{\mathbf{\mathring{B}}}_{0}-\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}\right)=n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{\otimes\mathbf{C}}_{i}\right)\mathbf{\tilde{\xi}}_{iq}^{\ast}+O_{p}\left(\frac{\sqrt{n}}{T}\right)\text{,}

(TS.8)

where
\mathbf{\tilde{\xi}}_{iq}^{\ast}=\mathbf{\bar{\xi}}_{iq}^{\ast}-E\left(\mathbf{\bar{\xi}}_{iq}^{\ast}\right)
, and
\mathbf{\bar{\xi}}_{iq}^{\ast}
is given by (recall
\mathbf{Z}_{i}^{\ast}=\mathbf{C}_{i}\left[T\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{s}_{i}\bar{\omega}_{i}}-E\left(T\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{s}_{i}\bar{\omega}_{i}}\right)\right]\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}
and
\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{s}_{i}\bar{\omega}_{i}}=T^{-1}q^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}\left(\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\ell}-\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\circ}\right)\left(\mathbf{\bar{\omega}}_{i\ell}-\mathbf{\bar{\omega}}_{i\circ}\right)^{\prime}
)

\mathbf{\bar{\xi}}_{iq}^{\ast}=q^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}vec\left[\left(\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\ell}-\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\circ}\right)\left(\mathbf{\bar{\omega}}_{i\ell}-\mathbf{\bar{\omega}}_{i\circ}\right)^{\prime}\right]=q^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}\left(\mathbf{\bar{\omega}}_{i\ell}\mathbf{\otimes\bar{s}}_{i\ell}\right)-\mathbf{\bar{\omega}}_{i\circ}\mathbf{\otimes\bar{s}}_{i\circ}\text{.}

Lemma
TS-2.4
established convergence in distribution for
n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{\otimes\mathbf{C}}_{i}\right)\mathbf{\tilde{\xi}}_{iq}^{\ast}
. Using
this Lemma in (
TS.8
), and noting that
d>1/2
implies
\sqrt{n}/T\rightarrow 0
as
n,T\rightarrow\infty
, we obtain (
TS.2
), as
required.

TS-2.1
Lemmas and their proofs for the case with interactive time
effects

Lemma TS-2.1

Consider
m\times m
matrices
\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{f}_{i}\bar{f}_{i}}=T^{-1}q^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}\mathbf{G}_{i}\left(\mathbf{\bar{f}}_{\ell}-\mathbf{\bar{f}}_{\circ}\right)\left(\mathbf{\bar{f}}_{\ell}-\mathbf{\bar{f}}_{\circ}\right)^{\prime}\mathbf{G}_{i}^{\prime}
,

\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{f}_{i}\bar{v}_{i}}=T^{-1}q^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}\mathbf{G}_{i}\left(\mathbf{\bar{f}}_{\ell}-\mathbf{\bar{f}}_{\circ}\right)\left(\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}-\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}\right)^{\prime}
and
\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{f}_{i}\bar{s}_{i}}=T^{-1}q^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}\mathbf{G}_{i}\left(\mathbf{\bar{f}}_{\ell}-\mathbf{\bar{f}}_{\circ}\right)\left(\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\ell}-\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\circ}\right)^{\prime}
,
where
\mathbf{\bar{f}}_{\ell}
and
\mathbf{\bar{f}}_{\circ}
are the
sub-sample and full sample time averages of
\mathbf{f}_{t}=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\mathbf{\Phi}_{f\ell}L^{\ell}\mathbf{\varepsilon}_{f,t-\ell}
,
\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\ell}
and
\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\circ}
are the
sub-sample and full sample time averages of the partial sum process
\mathbf{s}_{it}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{t}\mathbf{u}_{it}
, and
\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}

and
\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}
are the sub-sample and full sample time
averages of
\mathbf{v}_{it}=\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\ast}(L)\mathbf{u}_{it}=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\mathbf{C}_{ij}^{\ast}\mathbf{u}_{i,t-j}
.
Suppose Assumptions
1
,
2
and
4
hold, and
q
(
\geq 2
),
m
and
m_{f}
are fixed. Then,

\sup_{i}E\left\|\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{f}_{i}\bar{f}_{i}}\right\|=O\left(T^{-2}\right)\text{,}

(TS.9)

\sup_{i}E\left\|\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{f}_{i}\bar{v}_{i}}\right\|=O\left(T^{-2}\right)\text{,}

(TS.10)

and

\sup_{i}E\left\|\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{f}_{i}\bar{s}_{i}}\right\|=O\left(T^{-1}\right)\text{.}

(TS.11)

Proof.
Consider (
TS.9
) first. We can write
\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{f}_{i}\bar{f}_{i}}\,
as

\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{f}_{i}\bar{f}_{i}}=\mathbf{G}_{i}\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{f}\bar{f}}\mathbf{G}_{i}^{\prime}\text{,}

where

\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{f}\bar{f}}=T^{-1}q^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}\left(\mathbf{\bar{f}}_{\ell}-\mathbf{\bar{f}}_{\circ}\right)\left(\mathbf{\bar{f}}_{\ell}-\mathbf{\bar{f}}_{\circ}\right)^{\prime}\text{.}

Taking norm, expectation and
\sup
over
i
, we have

\sup_{i}E\left\|T\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{f}_{i}\bar{f}_{i}}\right\|\leq\left(\sup_{i}\left\|\mathbf{G}_{i}\right\|\right)^{2}E\left\|\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{f}\bar{f}}\right\|\leq K\cdot E\left\|T\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{f}\bar{f}}\right\|\text{,}

(TS.12)

where
\sup_{i}\left\|\mathbf{G}_{i}\right\|<K
by Assumption
4
. In addition,
\mathbf{f}_{t}
is a covariance stationary
process that satisfies essentially the same assumptions as the covariance
stationary process
\mathbf{v}_{it}
. Hence, using the same arguments as in
the proof of result (
A.9
) in Lemma
A.3
in Appendix, we obtain

E\left\|T\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{f}\bar{f}}\right\|\leq\sup_{\ell}E\left\|\mathbf{\bar{f}}_{\ell}\right\|^{2}+\sup_{i}E\left\|\mathbf{\bar{f}}_{\circ}\right\|^{2}=O\left(T^{-1}\right)\text{,}

(TS.13)

where

\sup_{\ell}E\left\|\mathbf{\bar{f}}_{\ell}\right\|^{2}=O\left(T^{-1}\right)\text{ and }\sup_{i}E\left\|\mathbf{\bar{f}}_{\circ}\right\|^{2}=O\left(T^{-1}\right)\text{.}

(TS.14)

Using (
TS.13
) in (
TS.12
), we obtain (
TS.9
).

Consider (
TS.10
) next. We can write
T\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{f}_{i}\bar{v}_{i}}\,
as

T\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{f}_{i}\bar{v}_{i}}=q^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}\mathbf{G}_{i}\left(\mathbf{\bar{f}}_{\ell}-\mathbf{\bar{f}}_{\circ}\right)\left(\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}-\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}\right)^{\prime}=q^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}\mathbf{G}_{i}\mathbf{\bar{f}}_{\ell}\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}^{\prime}-\mathbf{G}_{i}\mathbf{\bar{f}}_{\circ}\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}^{\prime}\text{.}

Hence

E\left\|T\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{f}_{i}\bar{v}_{i}}\right\|\leq q^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}\left\|\mathbf{G}_{i}\right\|\left(E\left\|\mathbf{\bar{f}}_{\ell}\right\|^{2}\right)^{1/2}\left(E\left\|\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\ell}\right\|^{2}\right)^{1/2}+\left\|\mathbf{G}_{i}\right\|\left(E\left\|\mathbf{\bar{f}}_{\circ}\right\|^{2}\right)^{1/2}\left(E\left\|\mathbf{\bar{v}}_{i\circ}\right\|^{2}\right)^{1/2}\text{.}

(TS.15)

Using (
A.15
), (
A.17
), (
TS.14
) and
\sup_{i}\left\|\mathbf{G}_{i}\right\|<K
(by Assumption
4
) in (
TS.15
),
result (
TS.10
) follows.

Consider the last result, (
TS.11
). Similarly to (
TS.15
), we have

E\left\|T\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{f}_{i}\bar{s}_{i}}\right\|\leq q^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}\left\|\mathbf{G}_{i}\right\|\left(E\left\|\mathbf{\bar{f}}_{\ell}\right\|^{2}\right)^{1/2}\left(E\left\|\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\ell}\right\|^{2}\right)^{1/2}+\left\|\mathbf{G}_{i}\right\|\left(E\left\|\mathbf{\bar{f}}_{\circ}\right\|^{2}\right)^{1/2}\left(E\left\|\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\circ}\right\|^{2}\right)^{1/2}\text{.}

(TS.16)

Using (
A.20
), (
TS.14
) and
\sup_{i}\left\|\mathbf{G}_{i}\right\|<K
(by Assumption
4
) in (
TS.16
), result
(
TS.11
) follows.

Lemma TS-2.2

Consider
\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{w}\bar{w}}=T^{-1}n^{-1}q^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}\left(\mathbf{\bar{w}}_{i\ell}-\mathbf{\bar{w}}_{i\circ}\right)\left(\mathbf{\bar{w}}_{i\ell}-\mathbf{\bar{w}}_{i\circ}\right)^{\prime}
and suppose the
m\times 1
vector
\mathbf{w}_{it}
is given by (
57
) featuring interactive time effects,
Assumptions
1
to
4
hold, and
q\left(\geq 2\right)
,
m
and
m_{f}
are fixed. Then

E\left|\mathbf{\beta}_{j0}^{\prime}\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{w}\bar{w}}\mathbf{\beta}_{j0}^{\prime}\right|=O\left(T^{-2}\right)\text{, for }j=1,2,...,r_{0}\text{,}

(TS.17)

where
\mathbf{\beta}_{j0}
(for
j=1,2,...,r_{0}
) are defined in
Assumption
3
.

Proof.
Premultiplying (
20
) by
\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}
, postmultiplying
by
\mathbf{B}_{0}
, and noting
\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{C}_{i}=\mathbf{0}
for
i=1,2,...,n
under Assumption
3
, we obtain

\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{w}\bar{w}}\mathbf{B}_{0}=\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{f}\bar{f}}\mathbf{B}_{0}+\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\left(\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{f}\bar{v}}+\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{v}\bar{f}}\right)\mathbf{B}_{0}+\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{v}\bar{v}}\mathbf{B}_{0}\text{,}

where by the orthonormality requirement
\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{B}_{0}=\mathbf{I}_{r_{0}}
. By Lemma
A.5
,
E\left\|\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{v}\bar{v}}\mathbf{B}_{0}\right\|=O\left(n^{-1/2}T^{-2}\right)
. Using results (
TS.9
) and (
TS.10
) of
Lemma
TS-2.1
, we obtain
E\left\|\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{f}\bar{f}}\mathbf{B}_{0}\right\|=O\left(T^{-2}\right)
and

E\left\|\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\left(\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{f}\bar{v}}+\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{v}\bar{f}}\right)\mathbf{B}_{0}\right\|\mathbf{=}O\left(T^{-2}\right)
. Hence, the dominant term is
O\left(T^{-2}\right)

and result (
TS.17
) follows.

Lemma TS-2.3

Consider
\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{w}\bar{w}}=T^{-1}n^{-1}q^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}\left(\mathbf{\bar{w}}_{i\ell}-\mathbf{\bar{w}}_{i\circ}\right)\left(\mathbf{\bar{w}}_{i\ell}-\mathbf{\bar{w}}_{i\circ}\right)^{\prime}
and the associated
m\times r
matrix
\mathbf{\hat{B}}_{0}
given by orthonormal eigenvectors of
\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{w}\bar{w}}
corresponding to its
r_{0}
smallest eigenvalues. Suppose the
m\times 1
vector
\mathbf{w}_{it}
is given by (
57
) featuring interactive
time effects, Assumptions
1
to
4
hold, and
q\left(\geq 2\right)
,
m
and
m_{f}
are fixed. Then

\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{w}\bar{w}}\mathbf{\hat{B}}_{0}=O_{p}\left(T^{-2}\right)\text{.}

(TS.18)

Proof.
Similarly to proof of result (
A.50
) in Lemma
A.7
in Appendix,
when
\mathbf{w}_{it}
is given by (
57
), we continue to have
0\leq tr\left(\mathbf{\hat{\Lambda}}\right)=tr\left(\mathbf{\hat{B}}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{w}\bar{w}}\mathbf{\hat{B}}_{0}\right)\leq tr\left(\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{w}\bar{w}}\mathbf{B}_{0}\right)
. But result (
TS.17
) of Lemma
TS-2.2
implies
E\left[tr\left(\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{w}\bar{w}}\mathbf{B}_{0}\right)\right]=O\left(T^{-2}\right)
and it follows
E\left\|\mathbf{\hat{\Lambda}}\right\|=O\left(T^{-2}\right)
. Taking norm of
\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{w}\bar{w}}\mathbf{\hat{B}}_{0}=\mathbf{\hat{B}}_{0}\mathbf{\Lambda}
and noting
\left\|\mathbf{\hat{B}}_{0}\right\|=1
, we
obtain
\left\|\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{w}\bar{w}}\mathbf{\hat{B}}_{0}\right\|\leq\left\|\mathbf{\hat{\Lambda}}\right\|
. Taking
expectations and using
E\left\|\mathbf{\hat{\Lambda}}\right\|=O\left(T^{-2}\right)
yields
E\left\|\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{w}\bar{w}}\mathbf{\hat{B}}_{0}\right\|=O\left(T^{-2}\right)
, which is
sufficient for (
TS.18
). This completes the proof.

Lemma TS-2.4

Suppose Assumptions
1
to
4
hold, and
q\left(\geq 2\right)
,
m
and
m_{f}
are fixed. Consider
\mathbf{\xi}_{iq}^{\ast}=q^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}\left(\mathbf{\bar{\omega}}_{i\ell}-\mathbf{\bar{\omega}}_{i}\right)\mathbf{\otimes}\left(\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\ell}-\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i}\right)
, where
\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\ell}
,
\mathbf{\bar{s}}_{i\circ}
are the sub-sample and full sample time averages
of the partial sum process
\mathbf{s}_{it}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{t}\mathbf{u}_{it}
, and
\mathbf{\bar{\omega}}_{i\ell}
,
\mathbf{\bar{\omega}}_{i}

are the sub-sample and full sample time averages of
\mathbf{\omega}_{it}=\mathbf{f}_{it}+\mathbf{v}_{it}
,
\mathbf{f}_{it}=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\mathbf{\Phi}_{fi\ell}L^{\ell}\mathbf{\varepsilon}_{f,t-\ell}
,
\mathbf{v}_{it}=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\mathbf{C}_{ij}^{\ast}\mathbf{u}_{i,t-j}
. Let
\mathbf{\Omega}_{\xi_{qi}^{\ast}}=Var\left(\mathbf{\bar{\xi}}_{iq}^{\ast}\right)
and suppose
\mathbf{\Omega}_{q}^{\ast}=\lim_{n,T\rightarrow\infty}\left(\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{\otimes\mathbf{C}}_{i}\right)\mathbf{\Omega}_{\xi_{qi}^{\ast}}\left(\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}\mathbf{\otimes\mathbf{C}}_{i}^{\prime}\right)
is positive definite. Then

n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\mathbf{\mathring{B}}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{\otimes\mathbf{C}}_{i}\right)\mathbf{\tilde{\xi}}_{iq}^{\ast}\rightarrow_{d}N\left(\mathbf{0,\Omega}_{q}^{\ast}\right),

(TS.19)

for
n,T\rightarrow\infty\,
jointly (in no particular order), where
\mathbf{\tilde{\xi}}_{iq}^{\ast}=\mathbf{\bar{\xi}}_{iq}^{\ast}-E\left(\mathbf{\bar{\xi}}_{iq}^{\ast}\right)
.

Proof.
(
TS.19
) can be established in a similar way as the proof of (
A.51
), but we rely on a central limit theorems for martingales (Theorem 24.3 in

?
)), since
\mathbf{\tilde{\xi}}_{iq}^{\ast}
is no longer independently
distributed over
i
in the presence of unobserved common factors.

wrds.wharton.upenn.edu, accessed on 2023-04-20. Specifically, our variables
come from annual “CRSP/Compustat Merged” database available through WRDS. We follow literature (
?
)) in constructing the variables listed in Table ES1.

Appendix ES-2
Data availability, variable construction and summary statistics

Data availability is summarized in Table ES2, for the three variable sets we
considered: {DO, TA}, {SD, LD, TA},{DO, BV, MV}. We apply following
data filters (in sequential order) to each of the variable set and data
sample separately.

Filter 1.

We omit firms with gaps in the data for the given variable
set and sample, and firms with
T_{i}<20
(unbalanced samples).

Filter 2.

We omit firms with nonpositive entries on any of the
variable in the given set and sample.

Filter 3.

We omit firms where, for a given sample and variable set,
average value of key ratios fall below 1 or above 99 percentiles estimated
after the application of the first two filters.

Filter 1 ensures that sufficient data exists, Filter 2 excludes firms with
negative or zero values, and Filter 3 is the outlier filter based on
percentiles of key ratios. Tables S3-S5 report summary statistics for each
variable set after all filters were applied.

Online Monte Carlo Supplement to

“Analysis of Multiple Long Run Relations
in Panel Data Models with Applications to Financial Ratios”
16
16

16

The views expressed in this paper are those of the authors and do not
necessarily reflect those of the Federal Reserve Bank of Dallas or the
Federal Reserve System. We gratefully acknowledge use of computational
resources provided by the Big-Tex High Performance Computing Group at the
Federal Reserve Bank of Dallas.

Alexander Chudik

Federal Reserve Bank of Dallas

University of Cambridge, UK and University of Southern California,

Birkbeck, University of London, United Kingdom

This online Monte Carlo supplement describes the Monte Carlo data
generating process in Section
MS-1
), provides the full set of the
corresponding Monte Carlo findings in Section
MS-2
, and
investigates sensitivity of
\tilde{r}
to differential scaling in Section
MS-3
.

Appendix MS-1
Description of Data Generating Processes

We have experiments with
r_{0}=0,1,2
long run relations, and with and
without interactive time effects. Overview af all experiments is provided in
the paper. Subsection
MS-1.1
below provides full details of the DGPs with
long run relations and without interactive time effects. Subsection
MS-1.2

provides details of the DGPs without long run relations and without
interactive time effects. Subsection
MS-1.3
provides details on
augmentation of each of these DGPs with interactive time effects.

MS-1.1
Experiments with
r_{0}=1
and
2
long run relations and
m=3

variables

We consider the following data generating process for
\mathbf{w}_{it}
,

\Delta\mathbf{w}_{it}=\mathbf{d}_{i}-\mathbf{\Pi}_{i}\mathbf{w}_{i,t-1}+\mathbf{u}_{it}-\mathbf{\Theta}_{i}\mathbf{u}_{i,t-1},

(MS.1)

where

\mathbf{\Pi}_{i}=\mathbf{A}_{i}\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime},

\Delta\mathbf{w}_{it}
is
m\times 1,

\mathbf{A}_{i}
is
m\times r_{0},

\mathbf{B}_{0}
is
m\times r_{0}.
To ensure that
\mathbf{w}_{it}
is not
trended we impose the restriction

\mathbf{d}_{i}=\mathbf{\Pi}_{i}\mathbf{\mu}_{iw}=\mathbf{A}_{i}\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{\mu}_{iw}.

(MS.2)

We set
m=3
and consider two cases, one and two long run relations,
r_{0}=1
and
r_{0}=2.

For
r_{0}=1

\mathbf{B}_{0}\mathbf{=\beta}_{0}=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}1\\
0\\
-1\end{array}\right)\text{.}

For
r_{0}=2

\mathbf{B}_{0}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathbf{\beta}_{10}&\mathbf{\beta}_{20}\end{array}\right)\mathbf{=}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}1&0\\
0&1\\
-1&-1\end{array}\right).

The VARMA representation of (
MS.1
) is given by

\mathbf{w}_{it}=\mathbf{d}_{i}+\left(\mathbf{I}_{m}-\mathbf{A}_{i}\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\right)\mathbf{w}_{i,t-1}+\mathbf{u}_{it}-\mathbf{\Theta}_{i}\mathbf{u}_{i,t-1}

(MS.3)

which can be written more compactly as

\mathbf{\Psi}_{i}(L)\mathbf{w}_{it}=\mathbf{d}_{i}+(\mathbf{I}_{m}-\mathbf{\Theta}_{i}L)\mathbf{u}_{it},

(MS.4)

where
\mathbf{\Psi}_{i}(L)=\mathbf{I}_{m}-\mathbf{\Psi}_{i}L,
and
\mathbf{\Psi}_{i}=\mathbf{I}_{m}-\mathbf{A}_{i}\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}
.

MS-1.1.1
Relation to Granger Representation

\Delta\mathbf{w}_{it}
can be written as

\Delta\mathbf{w}_{it}=\left[\mathbf{C}_{i}+(1-L)\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\ast}(L)\right]\mathbf{u}_{it}

(MS.5)

where
\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\ast}\left(L\right)=\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\mathbf{C}_{i\ell}^{\ast}L^{\ell}
. First differencing (
MS.4
),

\mathbf{\Psi}_{i}(L)\Delta\mathbf{w}_{it}=(\mathbf{I}_{m}-\mathbf{\Theta}_{i}L)\Delta\mathbf{u}_{it},

and using (
MS.5
), we obtain

\mathbf{\Psi}_{i}(L)\left[\mathbf{C}_{i}+(1-L)\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\ast}(L)\right]=(\mathbf{I}_{m}-\mathbf{\Theta}_{i}L)(1-L).

(MS.6)

Using the above we obtain
\mathbf{C}_{i}
and
\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\ast},

\ell=1,2,...
in terms of
\mathbf{\Psi}_{i}
and
\mathbf{\Theta}_{i}
.
Hence we can relate the parameters of (
MS.1
) to the general linear
representation

\mathbf{w}_{it}=\mathbf{a}_{i}+\mathbf{C}_{i}\mathbf{s}_{it}+\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\ast}(L)\mathbf{u}_{it}.

(MS.7)

where
\mathbf{s}_{it}=\mathbf{u}_{i1}+\mathbf{u}_{i2}+....+\mathbf{u}_{it}
. Pre-multiplying by
\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}
, we obtain

\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{w}_{it}=\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{a}_{i}+\mathbf{B_{0}^{\prime}C}_{i}^{\ast}(L)\mathbf{u}_{it}.

MS-1.1.2
Parameterization

We would expect
\mathbf{B}_{0}
to be more difficult to estimate the more
persistent is
\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{w}_{it}.
In the extreme case
where
\mathbf{A}_{i}=\mathbf{0},

\mathbf{B}_{0}
is not identified.
Pre-multiplying both sides of (
MS.1
) by
\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}

yields

\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\Delta\mathbf{w}_{it}=\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{d}_{i}-\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}_{i}\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{w}_{i,t-1}+\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{u}_{it}-\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{\Theta}_{i}\mathbf{u}_{i,t-1}.

(MS.8)

Let
\mathbf{\xi}_{it}=\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{w}_{it}
then

\mathbf{\xi}_{it}=\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{d}_{i}+(\mathbf{I}_{r}-\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}_{i})\mathbf{\xi}_{i,t-1}+\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{u}_{it}-\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{\Theta}_{i}\mathbf{u}_{i,t-1}.

(MS.9)

In the VAR(1) case where
\mathbf{\Theta}_{i}=\mathbf{0},
the persistence
of
\mathbf{\xi}_{it}=\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{w}_{it}
is fully
determined by the largest eigenvalue of
(\mathbf{I}_{r}-\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}_{i})
. Allowing for the MA component complicates
the analysis of persistence of
\mathbf{\xi}_{it}
, but it will still
depend on the size of the eigenvalues of
(\mathbf{I}_{r}-\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}_{i})
. As an approximation it is reasonable to
control the eigenvalues of
(\mathbf{I}_{r}-\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}_{i})
.

MS-1.1.3
Long run relations

When
r_{0}=1,
we set
\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}=\mathbf{\beta}_{0}^{\prime}=(1,0,-1)
and
\mathbf{A}_{i}=\left(a_{i,11},a_{i,21},a_{i,31}\right)^{\prime}
, and hence

\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}_{i}=a_{i,11}-a_{i,31}=\rho_{i}.

(MS.10)

In the case of VAR(1) the persistence of
\mathbf{\xi}_{it}
does not
depend on
a_{i,21}
, which we set to
0
, for all
i
. We consider two sets
of values for
\rho_{i}
and generate them as (for
i=1,2,...,n
)

\displaystyle\text{slow }\rho_{i}

\displaystyle\thicksim

\displaystyle IIDU\left[0.1,0.2\right]\text{, and}

\displaystyle\text{moderate }\rho_{i}

\displaystyle\thicksim

\displaystyle IIDU\left[0.1,0.3\right]\text{.}

Slow speed of convergence corresponds to a median half-life of 4.3 periods
(years) and moderate speed of convergence corresponds to a median half-life
of 3.1 periods (years). Since both
a_{i,11}
and
a_{i,31}
are nonzero,
long run causality runs from
w_{it,1}
to
\left(w_{it,2},w_{it,3}\right)

as well as from
w_{it,3}
to
w_{it,1}
. (
MS.10
) leaves us with one
free parameter to determine
\mathbf{A}_{i}
which we choose to control a
system measure of fit, as described below.

When
r_{0}=2,
we set the cointegrating vectors as

\mathbf{B}_{0}=\left(\mathbf{\beta}_{10},\mathbf{\beta}_{20}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}1&0\\
0&1\\
-1&-1\end{array}\right)\text{.}

Let

\mathbf{A}_{i}\mathbf{=}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}a_{i,11}&a_{i,12}\\
a_{i,21}&a_{i,22}\\
a_{i,31}&a_{i,32}\end{array}\right)\text{,}

then

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}_{2}-\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}_{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}_{2}-\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}1&0&-1\\
0&1&-1\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}a_{i,11}&a_{i,12}\\
a_{i,21}&a_{i,22}\\
a_{i,31}&a_{i,32}\end{array}\right)\text{,}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}1-\left(a_{i,11}-a_{i,31}\right)&-\left(a_{i,12}-a_{i,32}\right)\\
-\left(a_{i,21}-a_{i,31}\right)&1-\left(a_{i,22}-a_{i,32}\right)\end{array}\right)\text{.}

Hence

\mathbf{I}_{2}-\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}_{i}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}1-\rho_{i,11}&-\rho_{i,12}\\
-\rho_{i,21}&1-\rho_{i,22}\end{array}\right)\text{.}

(MS.20)

The eigenvalues of this matrix are given by

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1-\frac{1}{2}\rho_{i,11}-\frac{1}{2}\rho_{i,22}-\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\rho_{i,11}^{2}-2\rho_{i,11}\rho_{i,22}+\rho_{i,22}^{2}+4\rho_{i,12}\rho_{i,21}},

\displaystyle\lambda_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1-\frac{1}{2}\rho_{i,11}-\frac{1}{2}\rho_{i,22}+\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\rho_{i,11}^{2}-2\rho_{i,11}\rho_{i,22}+\rho_{i,22}^{2}+4\rho_{i,12}\rho_{i,21}}.

To simplify the design we set
\rho_{i,12}=\left(a_{i,12}-a_{i,32}\right)=0=\rho_{i,21}=\left(a_{i,21}-a_{i,31}\right)=0
. then

\rho_{i,11}=\left(a_{i,11}-a_{i,31}\right)\text{ and }\rho_{i,22}=\left(a_{i,22}-a_{i,32}\right),

and the eigenvalues are
\lambda_{1}=1-\rho_{i,11}
, and
\lambda_{2}=1-\rho_{i,22}
. A stable solution arises so long as
sup_{i,j}\left|1-\rho_{i,jj}\right|<1
. We consider two sets of
values for
\rho_{i,11}
and
\rho_{i,22}
and generate them as (for
i=1,2,...,n
)

\text{Slow }\rho_{i,11}\thicksim IIDU\left[0.1,0.2\right]\text{ ;
Moderate }\rho_{i,11}\thicksim IIDU\left[0.1,0.3\right],

\text{Slow }\rho_{i,22}\thicksim IIDU\left[0.1,0.2\right]\text{ ;
Moderate }\rho_{i,22}\thicksim IIDU\left[0.1,0.3\right].

We set
a_{i,j\ell}
values below, to achieve a balance between the speed
of convergence to equilibrium,
\rho_{ij}
, and the fit of the error
correction equations.

We generate
\mathbf{\mu}_{iw}=(\mu_{ij,w})
as
\mu_{ij,w}\thicksim IIDN(0,1)
, for
j=1,2,3;
and
i=1,2,...,n
. These parameters do not enter
the distribution of the estimators of
\mathbf{B}_{0}
.

For the error covariance matrix
\mathbf{V}_{i}=(\sigma_{i,pg})
we set
\sigma_{i,pp}=1
for
i=1,2,...,n
and
p=1,2,3
, and
\sigma_{i,pg}\thicksim IIDU(0,0.5)
, for
p\neq q\,
, and
i=1,2,...,n
.

For the MA part we consider two cases
\mathbf{\Theta}_{i}=0
(VAR design),
and

\mathbf{\Theta}_{i}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\theta_{i1}&0&0\\
0&\theta_{i2}&0\\
0&0&\theta_{i3}\end{array}\right)

with
\theta_{ij},

j=1,2,3

\sim U(0,0.50)
.

Initial values:
We first generate
\Delta\mathbf{w}_{it}
, for
t=1,2,...,T
, using the representation, (
MS.1
), and then cumulate
these differences to obtain
\mathbf{w}_{it}
. To this end we require the
initial values
\mathbf{B}^{\prime}\mathbf{w}_{i0}
and
\Delta\mathbf{w}_{i0}
. Using the Granger representation let
\Upsilon_{i}\left(L\right)=\left[\mathbf{C}_{i}+(1-L)\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\ast}(L)\right]=\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\Upsilon_{i\ell}L^{\ell}
, and

\Delta\mathbf{w}_{i0}=\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\Upsilon_{i,-\ell}\mathbf{u}_{i,-\ell}\text{,}

(MS.21)

and

\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{w}_{i0}=\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{a}_{i}+\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{C}_{i,-\ell}^{\ast}\mathbf{u}_{i,-\ell}\text{.}

Hence

E\left(\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{w}_{i0}\left|\mathbf{a}_{i}\right.\right)=E\left(\mathbf{\xi}_{i0}\left|\mathbf{a}_{i}\right.\right)=\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{a}_{i}

Also using (
MS.9
), and noting that
E\left(\Delta\mathbf{w}_{i,t-1}\right)=\mathbf{0}
, we have

E\left(\mathbf{\xi}_{it}\left|\mathbf{d}_{i},\mathbf{\alpha}_{i}\right.\right)=\mathbf{B}^{\prime}\mathbf{d}_{i}+(\mathbf{I}_{r}-\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}_{i})E\left(\mathbf{\xi}_{i,t-1}\left|\mathbf{\alpha}_{i}\right.\right),

and assuming
\left|\lambda_{\min}(\mathbf{I}_{r}-\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}_{i})\right|<1
we have

E\left(\mathbf{\xi}_{i0}\right)=(\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}_{i})^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{d}_{i}

Hence (Recall that
\mathbf{d}_{i}=\mathbf{\Pi}_{i}\mathbf{\mu}_{iw}
, see
(
MS.2
))

\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{w}_{i0}=\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{\mu}_{iw}+\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{C}_{i,-\ell}^{\ast}\mathbf{u}_{i,-\ell}\text{.}

Using (
MS.7
) we also have

Var\left(\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{w}_{it}\right)=Var\left(\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{w}_{i0}\right)=\mathbf{B_{0}^{\prime}}\left(\sum_{s=0}^{\infty}\mathbf{C}_{is}^{\ast\prime}\mathbf{V}_{i}\mathbf{C}_{is}^{\ast}\right)\mathbf{B}_{0}.

(MS.22)

We generate
\Delta\mathbf{w}_{i0}
and
\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{w}_{i0}
according to

\Delta\mathbf{w}_{i0}=\sum_{\ell=0}^{M}\Upsilon_{i,-\ell}\mathbf{u}_{i,-\ell}\text{, and }\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{w}_{i0}=\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{\mu}_{iw}+\sum_{\ell=0}^{M}\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{C}_{i,-\ell}^{\ast}\mathbf{u}_{i,-\ell}\text{,}

where we set
M=50
.

MS-1.1.4
Fit of error correction equations and setting the error
correction coefficients

An average measure of fit for the
m\times 1
system of equations for
\Delta\mathbf{w}_{it}=(\Delta\mathring{w}_{it,1},\Delta\mathring{w}_{it,2},...,\Delta\mathring{w}_{it,m})^{\prime}
is given by

PR_{nT}^{2}=1-\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{i=1}^{n}u_{it,j}^{2}}{\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\Delta w_{it,j}-\Delta\bar{w}_{iT,j})^{2}},

(MS.23)

where
\Delta\bar{w}_{iT,j}=T^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Delta w_{it,j}
. This
system measure places equal weights on the fit of the
m
different
error-correcting (EC) equations. We can control for
PR_{nT}^{2}
by
generating remaining free parameters in
\mathbf{A}_{i}
.

MS-1.1.5
Case of
r_{0}=1
long run relation in VAR(1) design

Using (
MS.23
), and for
\mathbf{\Theta}_{i}=\mathbf{0}
, under
stationarity and for large
n
, (
T
does not need to be large given the
cross-sectional independence of
\mathbf{u}_{it}
) we have

\displaystyle PR_{n}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1-\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sum_{i=1}^{n}E\left(u_{it,j}^{2}\right)}{\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sum_{i=1}^{n}E(\Delta w_{it,j}-\Delta\bar{w}_{iT,j})^{2}}

(MS.24)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}tr\left(\mathbf{A}_{i}\mathbf{\Omega}_{i}\mathbf{A}_{i}^{\prime}\right)}{\sum_{i=1}^{n}tr\left(\mathbf{A}_{i}\mathbf{\Omega}_{i}\mathbf{A}_{i}^{\prime}\right)+\sum_{i=1}^{n}tr(\mathbf{V}_{i})},

where
\mathbf{\Omega}_{i}=Var\left(\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{w}_{i,t-1}\right)
. Using (
MS.9
) (when
\mathbf{\Theta}_{i}=\mathbf{0}
)we note that

\mathbf{\xi}_{it}=\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{w}_{i,t-1}=\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{d}_{i}+(\mathbf{I}_{r}-\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}_{i})\mathbf{\xi}_{i,t-1}+\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{u}_{it},

and
\mathbf{\Omega}_{i}
is given by

\mathbf{\Omega}_{i}=(\mathbf{I}_{r}-\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}_{i})\mathbf{\Omega}_{i}(\mathbf{I}_{r}-\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}_{i})^{\prime}+\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{V}_{i}\mathbf{B}_{0}.

(MS.25)

In the case where
r_{0}=1
,
\mathbf{\Omega}_{i}
is a scalar,
\mathbf{B}_{0}=\mathbf{\beta}_{1,0}
, and we have

\mathbf{\Omega}_{i}=\frac{\mathbf{\beta}_{1,0}^{\prime}\mathbf{V}_{i}\mathbf{\beta}_{1,0}.}{1-(1-\rho_{i})^{2}},

Hence

PR_{n}^{2}=\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\left(\mathbf{A}_{i}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}_{i}\right)\mathbf{\beta}_{1,0}^{\prime}\mathbf{V}_{i}\mathbf{\beta}_{1,0}}{1-(1-\rho_{i})^{2}}}{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\left(\mathbf{A}_{i}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}_{i}\right)\mathbf{\beta}_{1,0}^{\prime}\mathbf{V}_{i}\mathbf{\beta}_{1,0}}{1-(1-\rho_{i})^{2}}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}tr(\mathbf{V}_{i})}.

For a given value of
\rho_{i}
, it is now possible to use
\mathbf{A}_{i}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}_{i}=a_{i,11}^{2}+\alpha_{i,21}^{2}+\alpha_{i,31}^{2}
as a scaling factor to achieve a desired value of
PR_{n}^{2}
.
But we need to take account of the fact that
\rho_{i}=a_{i,11}-a_{i,31}

and both
a_{i,11}
and
a_{i,31}
can not be scaled up. Given
\rho_{i}

we set

\mathbf{A}_{i}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}_{i}=\left(\rho_{i}+a_{i,31}\right)^{2}+a_{i,21}^{2}+a_{i,31}^{2}=\varkappa^{2},

and then derive
a_{i,21}
and
a_{i,31}
that satisfy the above equation.
Then
\varkappa^{2}
can be set in terms of
PR_{n}^{2}:

\varkappa^{2}=\left(\frac{1-PR_{n}^{2}}{PR_{n}^{2}}\right)\left(\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}tr(\mathbf{V}_{i})}{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\mathbf{\beta}_{1,0}^{\prime}\mathbf{V}_{i}\mathbf{\beta}_{1,0}}{1-(1-\rho_{i})^{2}}}\right).

(MS.26)

We can allow variations across
a_{i,31}
and
a_{i,21}
, so long as
\left(\rho_{i}+a_{i,31}\right)^{2}+a_{i,21}^{2}+a_{i,31}^{2}=\varkappa^{2}
. To check the feasibility of the above procedure (results in real-valued
a_{i,j\ell}
) we set
a_{i,21}=0
, and note that
a_{i,31}
must now
satisfy the quadratic equation

a_{i,31}^{2}+\rho_{i}a_{i,31}+\frac{1}{2}\left(\rho_{i}^{2}-\varkappa^{2}\right)=0.

For this equation to have real solutions we must have
\rho_{i}^{2}-2(\rho_{i}^{2}-\varkappa^{2})>0
, or
\varkappa^{2}>\sup_{i}\rho_{i}^{2}/2
. We
consider two values of
PR_{n}^{2}=0.20
and
0.30
.

MS-1.1.6
Case of
r_{0}=2
long run relations in VAR(1) design

Setting
\mathbf{Q}_{i}=(\mathbf{I}_{r}-\mathbf{BA}_{i})
,
and
using (
MS.25
), we have
17
17

17

Note that
vec\left(\mathbf{ABC}\right)=\left(\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\otimes\mathbf{A}\right)vec\left(\mathbf{B}\right)
.

vec\left(\mathbf{\Omega}_{i}\right)=\left(\mathbf{Q}_{i}\otimes\mathbf{Q}_{i}\right)vec\left(\mathbf{\Omega}_{i}\right)+vec\left(\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{V}_{i}\mathbf{B}_{0}\right)

Then

vec\left(\mathbf{\Omega}_{i}\right)=\left[\mathbf{I}_{r^{2}}-\left(\mathbf{Q}_{i}\otimes\mathbf{Q}_{i}\right)\right]^{-1}vec\left(\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{V}_{i}\mathbf{B}_{0}\right).

Also since

tr\left(\mathbf{A}_{i}\mathbf{\Omega}_{i}\mathbf{A}_{i}^{\prime}\right)=tr\left(\mathbf{\Omega}_{i}\mathbf{A}_{i}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}_{i}\right)=vec\left(\mathbf{A}_{i}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}_{i}\right)^{\prime}vec\left(\mathbf{\Omega}_{i}\right),

then

tr\left(\mathbf{A}_{i}\mathbf{\Omega}_{i}\mathbf{A}_{i}^{\prime}\right)=vec\left(\mathbf{A}_{i}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}_{i}\right)^{\prime}\left[\mathbf{I}_{r^{2}}-\left(\mathbf{Q}_{i}\otimes\mathbf{Q}_{i}\right)\right]^{-1}vec\left(\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{V}_{i}\mathbf{B}_{0}\right),

\mathbf{I}_{r^{2}}-\left(\mathbf{Q}_{i}\otimes\mathbf{Q}_{i}\right)=diag(1-\rho_{i,11}^{2},1-\rho_{i,11}\rho_{i,22},1-\rho_{i,11}\rho_{i,22},1-\rho_{i,22}^{2})\text{.}

As before
\mathbf{A}_{i}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}_{i}
can be scaled up/down
fixing the value of
\mathbf{Q}_{i}
. Recall from (
MS.20
) that (since
\rho_{i,12}=\left(a_{i,12}-a_{i,32}\right)=0
, and
\rho_{i,21}=\left(a_{i,21}-a_{i,31}\right)=0)
,

\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{i}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\rho_{i,11}&0\\
0&\rho_{i,22}\end{array}\right)

where
\rho_{i,11}=\left(a_{i,11}-a_{i,31}\right)
and
\rho_{i,22}=\left(a_{i,32}-a_{i,22}\right)
. Noting that

PR_{n}^{2}=\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}tr\left(\mathbf{A}_{i}\mathbf{\Omega}_{i}\mathbf{A}_{i}^{\prime}\right)}{\sum_{i=1}^{n}tr\left(\mathbf{A}_{i}\mathbf{\Omega}_{i}\mathbf{A}_{i}^{\prime}\right)+\sum_{i=1}^{n}tr(\mathbf{V}_{i})},

then

\frac{1-PR_{n}^{2}}{PR_{n}^{2}}=\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}tr(\mathbf{V}_{i})}{\sum_{i=1}^{n}vec\left(\mathbf{A}_{i}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}_{i}\right)^{\prime}\left[\mathbf{I}_{r^{2}}-\left(\mathbf{Q}_{i}\otimes\mathbf{Q}_{i}\right)\right]^{-1}vec\left(\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{V}_{i}\mathbf{B}_{0}\right)}\text{,}

where

\displaystyle\mathbf{A}_{i}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}_{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}a_{i,11}&a_{i,21}&a_{i,31}\\
a_{i,12}&a_{i,22}&a_{i,32}\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}a_{i,11}&a_{i,12}\\
a_{i,21}&a_{i,22}\\
a_{i,31}&a_{i,32}\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\sum_{s=1}^{3}a_{i,s1}^{2}&\sum_{s=1}^{3}a_{i,s1}a_{i,s2}\\
\sum_{s=1}^{3}a_{i,s1}a_{i,s2}&\sum_{s=1}^{3}a_{i,s2}^{2}\end{array}\right).

As before
\rho_{i,11}
and
\rho_{i,22}
are pre-set:

\text{Slow }\rho_{i,11}\thicksim IIDU\left[0.1,0.2\right]\text{ ;
Moderate }\rho_{i,11}\thicksim IIDU\left[0.1,0.3\right],

\text{Slow }\rho_{i,22}\thicksim IIDU\left[0.1,0.2\right]\text{ ;
Moderate }\rho_{i,22}\thicksim IIDU\left[0.1,0.3\right].

and
\rho_{i,12}=\left(a_{i,12}-a_{i,32}\right)=0=\rho_{i,21}=\left(a_{i,21}-a_{i,31}\right)=0
. Overall, we have the following restrictions

\displaystyle a_{i,11}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle a_{i,31}+\rho_{i,11}\text{, and }a_{i,22}=a_{i,32}+\rho_{i,22}

\displaystyle a_{i,12}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle a_{i,32}\text{ and }a_{i,21}=a_{i,31}

We are left with two free parameters to control the fit of the model. To
this end note that
.

\mathbf{A}_{i}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}_{i}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}{\small(a}_{i,31}{\small+\rho}_{i,11}{\small)}^{2}{\small+2a}_{i,31}^{2}&\left(a_{i,31}+\rho_{i,11}\right){\small a}_{i,32}{\small+a}_{i,31}\left(a_{i,32}+\rho_{i,22}\right){\small+a}_{i,31}{\small a}_{i,32}\\
\left(a_{i,31}+\rho_{i,11}\right){\small a}_{i,32}{\small+a}_{i,31}\left(a_{i,32}+\rho_{i,22}\right){\small+a}_{i,31}{\small a}_{i,32}&{\small(a}_{i,32}{\small+\rho}_{i,22}{\small)}^{2}{\small+2a}_{i,32}^{2}\end{array}\right),

and
\mathbf{A}_{i}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}_{i}
can scaled up by scaling on
a_{i,31}
and
a_{i,32}.
We set
a_{i,31}=a_{i,32}=\varkappa
which yields

\mathbf{A}_{i}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}_{i}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}{\small\rho}_{i,11}^{2}{\small+2\varkappa\rho}_{i,11}{\small+3\varkappa}^{2}&\left(\rho_{i,11}+\rho_{i,22}\right){\small\varkappa+3\varkappa}^{2}\\
\left(\rho_{i,11}+\rho_{i,22}\right){\small\varkappa+3\varkappa}^{2}&{\small\rho}_{i,22}^{2}{\small+2\varkappa\rho}_{i,22}{\small+3\varkappa}^{2}\end{array}\right).

Then we solve the following equation for
\varkappa
that yields a desired
value of
PR_{n}^{2}

\frac{1-PR_{n}^{2}}{PR_{n}^{2}}=\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}tr(\mathbf{V}_{i})}{\sum_{i=1}^{n}vec\left(\mathbf{A}_{i}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}_{i}\right)\left[\mathbf{I}_{r^{2}}-\left(\mathbf{Q}_{i}\otimes\mathbf{Q}_{i}\right)\right]^{-1}vec\left(\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}\mathbf{V}_{i}\mathbf{B}_{0}\right)},

where

\left[\mathbf{I}_{r^{2}}-\left(\mathbf{Q}_{i}\otimes\mathbf{Q}_{i}\right)\right]^{-1}=diag(\frac{1}{1-\rho_{i,11}^{2}},\frac{1}{1-\rho_{i,11}\rho_{i,22}},\frac{1}{1-\rho_{i,11}\rho_{i,22}},\frac{1}{1-\rho_{i,22}^{2}})\text{.}

We consider the same two values of
PR_{n}^{2}=0.20
and
0.30
.

MS-1.1.7
Case of VARMA(1,1) design

When
\mathbf{\Theta}_{i}\neq\mathbf{0}
, it is cumbersome to solve for
PR_{n}^{2}
analytically. We proceed by computing
\varkappa
using
stochastic simulations to ensure desired value of
PR_{RnT}^{2}\left(\varkappa\right)
, where

PR_{RnT}^{2}\left(\varkappa\right)=\frac{1}{R}\sum_{rep=1}^{R}PR_{nT}^{2}\left(rep,\varkappa\right)\text{,}

and

PR_{nT}^{2}\left(rep,\varkappa\right)=1-\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(u_{it,j}^{\left(rep\right)}\right)^{2}}{\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\Delta w_{it,j}^{\left(rep\right)}-\Delta\bar{w}_{iT,j}^{\left(rep\right)})^{2}}\text{,}

in which we use
rep=1,2,...,R
to denote individual MC replications (
R=2000
). We solve
PR_{RnT}^{2}\left(\varkappa\right)=0.2
or
0.3

using grid search method.

MS-1.2
Experiments with no long run relations

Experiment with
I\left(1\right)
variables and
r_{0}=0
are based on the
DGP given by

\Delta\mathbf{w}_{it}=\mathbf{\Phi}_{i}\Delta\mathbf{w}_{i,t-1}+\mathbf{u}_{it},

for
i=1,2,...,n
,
t=1,2,...,T
, where
\mathbf{u}_{it}=\mathbf{P}_{i}\mathbf{\varepsilon}_{it}
,
\mathbf{\varepsilon}_{it}\thicksim IIDN(\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{m})
, the error covariance matrix
\mathbf{P}_{i}\mathbf{P}_{i}^{\prime}\equiv\mathbf{\Sigma}_{ui}=\left[\sigma_{i,\ell q}\right]
, is generated using
\sigma_{i,\ell\ell}=1
for
i=1,2,...,n

and
\ell=1,2,3
, and
\sigma_{i,\ell q}\thicksim IIDU(0,0.5)
, for
\ell\neq q\,
, and
i=1,2,...,n
. Matrix
\mathbf{\Phi}_{i}
is diagonal with
\phi_{ij}
elements on the diagonal, for
j=1,2,...,m
. We consider three
options for
\phi_{ij}
: (
i
) low values
\phi_{ij}\sim U[0,0.8]
, (
ii
)
moderate values
\phi_{ij}\sim U[0.7,0.9]
, and (
iii
) high values
\phi_{ij}\sim U[0.80,0.95]
. Initial values are generated as
\Delta w_{i0,j}\sim IIDN\left[0,\left(1-\phi_{ij}^{2}\right)^{-1}\right]
for
j=1,2,...,m
, and
i=1,2,...,n
, and
\mathbf{w}_{i,-1}
is set to
\mathbf{0}
. This DGP is also a special case of (
1
). Specifically, it leads
to
\mathbf{w}_{it}=\mathbf{w}_{i0}+\mathbf{G}_{i}\mathbf{f}_{t}+\mathbf{C}_{i}\mathbf{s}_{it}+\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\ast}(L)\mathbf{u}_{it},
where
\mathbf{C}_{i}=\left(\mathbf{I}_{m}-\mathbf{\Phi}_{i}\right)^{-1}
, and
\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\ast}(L)=-\mathbf{\Phi}_{i}\left(\mathbf{I}_{m}-\mathbf{\Phi}_{i}\right)^{-1}\left(\mathbf{I}_{m}-\mathbf{\Phi}_{i}L\right)^{-1}
.

MS-1.3
Experiments with interactive time effects

We augment the general linear process versions of the above VARMA and VAR
specifications with
\mathbf{G}_{i}\mathbf{f}_{t}
, namely

\mathbf{w}_{it}=\mathbf{w}_{i0}+\mathbf{G}_{i}\mathbf{f}_{t}+\mathbf{C}_{i}\mathbf{s}_{it}+\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\ast}(L)\mathbf{u}_{it},

(MS.29)

where
\mathbf{C}_{i}
and
\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\ast}(L)
are obtained from the
VARMA and VAR models above.
\mathbf{f}_{t}
is an
m_{f}\times 1\,\
vector
of unobserved common factors,
\mathbf{G}_{i}
is an
m\times m_{f}
matrix
of factor loadings. We set
m_{f}=4
and generate latent factors,
\mathbf{f}_{t}
, to be serially correlated with a break,

\displaystyle\mathbf{f}_{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\rho_{f_{1}}\mathbf{f}_{t-1}+\sqrt{1-\rho_{f1}^{2}}\mathbf{v}_{ft}\text{, for }t=1,2,...,[T/2]-1\text{,}

\displaystyle\mathbf{f}_{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\rho_{f2}\mathbf{f}_{t-1}+\sqrt{1-\rho_{f2}^{2}}\mathbf{v}_{t}\text{, for }t=[T/2],[T/2]+1,...,T-1\text{,}

where
\rho_{f1}=0.6
,
\rho_{f2}=0.4
, and
\mathbf{v}_{t}\sim IIDN\left(\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{m_{f}}\right)
. Individual elements of
\mathbf{G}_{i}
are generated as
IIDU\left[0.0.4\right]
.

MS-1.4
Design from Chudik, Pesaran and Smith (2021)

This design features
m=2
variables in
\mathbf{w}_{it}=\left(w_{1,it},w_{2,it}\right)^{\prime}
, generated according to the
cross-sectionally independent DGP described in Section 3.1 of

?
). In this design,
\mathbf{w}_{it}
is generated as

\displaystyle\Delta w_{1,it}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c_{i}-a_{i}\left(w_{1,i,t-1}-w_{2,i,t-1}\right)+u_{1,it}\text{,}

(MS.30)

\displaystyle\Delta w_{2,it}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle u_{2,it}\text{,}

(MS.31)

where
a_{i}\sim IIDU\left[0.2,0.3\right]
,
u_{1,it}=\sigma_{1i}e_{1,it}
,
u_{2,it}=\sigma_{2i}e_{2,it}
,
\sigma_{1,i}^{2},\sigma_{2,i}^{2}\sim IIDU\left[0.8,1.2\right]
,

\left(\begin{array}[]{c}e_{1,it}\\
e_{2,it}\end{array}\right)\sim IIDN\left(\mathbf{0}_{2},\mathbf{\Sigma}_{e}\right)\text{, }\mathbf{\Sigma}_{e}\sim\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}1&\rho_{ei}\\
\rho_{ei}&1\end{array}\right)\text{, and }\rho_{ei}\sim IIDU\left[0.3,0.7\right]\text{.}

Full details of this design are provided in Section 3.1 of

?
).

Appendix MS-3
Sensitivity of
\tilde{r}
to scaling

It is clear that eigenvalues of
\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{w}\bar{w}}
depend on the
scale of the observations,
\mathbf{w}_{it}=\left(w_{it,1},w_{it,2},...,w_{it,m}\right)^{\prime}
, and some form of scaling
of data is required to reduce or eliminate the sensitivity of the estimator
of
r_{0}
to scaling. In Section
8
in the body of the paper,
we proposed using eigenvalues of the correlation matrix
\mathbf{R}_{{}_{\bar{w}\bar{w}}}
given by (
63
), which, for convenience, we reproduce here:

\mathbf{R}_{{}_{\bar{w}\bar{w}}}=\left[diag\left(\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{w}\bar{w}}\right)\right]^{-1/2}\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{w}\bar{w}}\left[diag\left(\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{w}\bar{w}}\right)\right]^{-1/2}.

Accordingly, we defined in equation (
64
) the following estimator of

r_{0}
,

\tilde{r}=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mathcal{I}\left(\tilde{\lambda}_{j}<T^{-\delta}\right),

where
\tilde{\lambda}_{j}
, for
j=1,2,...,m
are the eigenvalues of
\mathbf{R}_{{}_{\bar{w}\bar{w}}}
.

Consider the scaled vector
\mathbf{\dot{w}}_{it}=\mathbf{Dw}_{it}
, where
\mathbf{D}=diag\left(d_{11},d_{22},...,d_{mm}\right)
is a diagonal
m\times m
scaling matrix with
d_{kk}>0
for all
k=1,2,...,m
. Then matrix

\mathbf{\dot{R}}_{{}_{\bar{w}\bar{w}}}
computed based on scaled variables
\mathbf{\dot{w}}_{it}
remains unaffected by the scaling matrix
\mathbf{D}
, namely
\mathbf{R}_{{}_{\bar{w}\bar{w}}}=\mathbf{\dot{R}}_{{}_{\bar{w}\bar{w}}}
. However, this no longer true when differential scaling is considered,
given by
\mathbf{\ddot{w}}_{it}=\mathbf{D}_{i}\mathbf{w}_{it}
, where
\mathbf{D}_{i}=diag\left(d_{i,11},d_{i,22},...,d_{i,mm}\right)
, for
i=1,2,...,n
, are unit-specific diagonal scaling matrices with
d_{i,kk}>0

for all
k=1,2,...,m
and
i=1,2,...,n
. In this case, matrix
\mathbf{\ddot{R}}_{{}_{\bar{w}\bar{w}}}
, computed based on
\mathbf{\ddot{w}}_{it}
, is
affected by scaling, namely
\mathbf{\ddot{R}}_{{}_{\bar{w}\bar{w}}}\neq\mathbf{R}_{{}_{\bar{w}\bar{w}}}
in the general case where
\mathbf{D}_{i}

differs over
i
.

To investigate impact of differential scaling given by
\mathbf{\ddot{w}}_{it}=\mathbf{D}_{i}\mathbf{w}_{it}
on the small sample performance of
\tilde{r}
, we consider additional Monte Carlo experiments below. We
generate
\mathbf{D}_{i}=\varkappa_{i}\mathbf{I}_{3}
, for
i=1,2,...,n
,
where
\varkappa_{i}\sim IIDU\left[1,2\right]
. We compare the small
sample performance of
\tilde{r}
based on the original data
\mathbf{w}_{it}
and the scaled data
\mathbf{\ddot{w}}_{it}=\mathbf{D}_{i}\mathbf{w}_{it}=\varkappa_{i}\mathbf{w}_{it}
, for three experiments. Table MS101
reports selection frequencies for the estimation of
r_{0}
using
\tilde{r}

based on original and scaled data as well as two choices of
q=2
and
4

and two choices of exponent
\delta=1/4
and
1/2
, in experiments with
r_{0}=0
(no long run relations), given by the first-differenced VAR(1)
design (
69
) with high persistence
\phi_{i,\ell\ell}\sim U[0.80,0.95]
. Table MS102 reports the same set of results in the case of
VAR(1) experiments with
r_{0}=1
long-run relationship, interactive
effects, low speed of convergence, low value of
PR^{2}=0.2
and chi-squared
distributed errors. Table MS103 below reports findings for VAR(1)
experiments with
r_{0}=2
long-run relations, interactive effects, low
speed of convergence, low value of
PR^{2}=0.2
and chi-squared distributed
errors. Overall, these Monte Carlo findings show a very small dependence of
\tilde{r}
on the adopted differential scaling. In addition, these findings
also show that performance of
\tilde{r}
is almost identical for the two
choices of
q=2
and
4
.
